  - **No daily puzzle at all anymore (2nd pass)** — originally added as a "New Puzzle (practice)" side door alongside a still-primary date-seeded daily target (`gameUtils.pickDailyEntry`/`todayDateString`, seeded via `hashString`); the user found the whole day-locked framing pointless for this game and asked to just always be able to play again. `pickDailyEntry`/`todayDateString` were deleted from `gameUtils.js` entirely (nothing else used them; `hashString`/`makeSeededRng` stayed — both are still used elsewhere, e.g. `connectionsGenerator.test.js`'s deterministic-rng pattern). `target` is now a plain `data` field (not derived from a date), picked via `Math.random()` over `eligibleGameEntries` by `startNewPuzzle()` — the SAME method used for both "give me a new one" and the initial pick. There's no cap on how many rounds can be played: no Firebase writes happen per round at all (this game only ever touches `localStorage`), so nothing gates repeated play except however many distinct movies are in the library to draw from.
  - **Persistence changed from date-keyed to a single fixed key** (`cinemaRoll.reelWordle.current`, replacing `cinemaRoll.reelWordle.<dateString>`) storing `{ targetKey, guessedKeys }` — a reload resumes the current in-progress round (by looking up `targetKey` in the library), rather than a specific day's puzzle. `eligibleGameEntries` is watched (`immediate: true`) rather than lifecycle-hooked directly, since the library may still be loading when the component mounts; the handler only acts if `!this.target`, so a library change mid-round (e.g. rating a new movie) never yanks out from under an in-progress puzzle.
  - **Mobile layout fix, two iterations (Jul 2026, bug reports)**: the guess history used to be a 7-column CSS grid (`grid-template-columns: 2fr repeat(6, 1fr)`) with a hardcoded `min-width: 480px`, wider than a phone screen — every guess row scrolled sideways to be read. First fix replaced it with a `flex-wrap` row of labeled chips (`.clue-chip`), which killed the scroll but introduced a new problem: 6 variable-length chips still wrapped to a visible 2nd line on a phone, which looked worse per follow-up feedback ("need to fit across one screen"). **Current design**: `.clue-cells` is a fixed CSS Grid, `grid-template-columns: repeat(6, 1fr)` with `min-width: 0` on each `.clue-cell` (the classic grid/flexbox gotcha — without it, a cell's min-width defaults to its content size and overrides any truncation) and `.clue-value` set to `overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; white-space: nowrap`. This guarantees exactly one line at ANY width — long director names or genre lists truncate instead of wrapping OR forcing scroll (full text is on the `title` attribute). Each cell carries its own abbreviated label (Yr/Dec/Dir/Gen/Run/Rtg) since there's no room for a separate header row anymore.
  - **Input position, REVERSED again (Jul 2026, two rounds of bug reports — see below for the current, actually-landed design)**: first moved BELOW the guess history ("so I can see the most recent information as I'm typing"), then the user changed their mind back to input-at-top but with the list itself flipped so new entries push old ones down rather than requiring a scroll. The below-history version is history now, not the current layout — see the next bullet for what's actually live.
  - **Follow-up polish pass (Jul 2026, 4 bug reports filed right after the redesign above shipped)**:
    - **Full-word labels, not abbreviations**: `.clue-label` text changed from Yr/Dec/Dir/Gen/Run/Rtg to Year/Decade/Director/Genre/Runtime/Rating — the abbreviations weren't self-explanatory. Still fits the fixed 6-column grid at typical phone widths (verified — nothing reintroduced the horizontal-scroll bug this whole redesign exists to prevent); `.clue-label` also picked up the same `overflow:hidden/ellipsis/nowrap` treatment `.clue-value` already had, as a safety net against wrapping on very narrow screens.
    - **"New Puzzle" moved to the top** of the page (right after the subtitle, above target-clues), not the bottom — was easy to miss/required scrolling past the whole guess history to reach.
    - **Spacing fix**: `.clue-grid` picked up `margin-bottom: 1rem` — the input was rendering flush against the last guess row's cells ("squished") once the input moved below the history (previous bullet).
    - **Genre color ambiguity resolved with an explicit fraction, not just color**: a guess whose genre SET is an exact match (green) vs. one that merely shares some genres (yellow, `.partial`) rendered THE SAME shared-genre text, differing only by color — not legible/understandable on its own ("I guessed Robot Dreams... Drama in Yellow... Aftersun... Drama... in Green, what's the difference"). `wordleClues.js`'s `genres` object gained a `total` field (`Math.max(guessGenres.length, targetGenres.length)` — `max`, not just the target's count, so a guess with MORE genres than the target — a real partial case, not `allMatch` — doesn't misleadingly compute as a "complete" fraction). The clue value now renders e.g. `"Drama (1/2)"` vs `"Drama (2/2)"`, so the number — not just the color — tells you whether it's exact.
    - **Direct "back to search" shortcut**: a new `.home-shortcut` button (home icon, top-right corner) pushes straight to `/` — see "Resume-last-game + preserved search" above for the matching `beforeRouteLeave` widening that makes this actually preserve your search instead of landing on a blank Home.
  - **Input pinned at the top, guess list grows upward (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — "I've changed my mind again... I want the stack to also grow up not down")**: reverses the previous bullet's below-history placement one round later. Current template order: "New Puzzle" → target-clues → the input (`.guess-form` / win banner) → `.clue-grid`. The `guesses` array itself stays append-only/chronological (untouched — persistence, `score`, `status` etc. all key off it and don't care about order); a new `displayGuesses` computed (`[...guesses].reverse()`) is what the template actually iterates, keyed by `clue.entryKey` (not array index, since index-as-key would be unstable across a reversed, growing list — every existing row's "position" changes each time a new one is prepended). Net effect: the input never moves down the page, and each new guess appears directly below it, pushing earlier ones further down — addresses both this request and an adjacent one ("the hints... I didn't see them there") from the same round, since target-clues sits right next to the input again instead of far from it.
- **Connections** (`connectionsGenerator.js` + `ConnectionsGame.vue`) — NYT Connections-style: find 4 groups of 4 movies each sharing an attribute. `buildCandidateCategories` finds every value shared by 4+ library movies; `generateConnectionsPuzzle` shuffles those candidates and greedily claims 4 with **no movie assigned to more than one group's answer key** (a movie CAN be individually eligible for several candidates at once — that's real puzzle difficulty, not a bug — the constraint is only on which category actually *claims* it). **Category set changed (Jul 2026)** per feedback: production companies dropped ("too tricky" — a shared studio rarely feels like a meaningful connection the way a shared director/cast member does); **keywords added** in their place via `computeFlatKeywords` (already-existing, synchronous), capped to `KEYWORD_MAX_MOVIE_COUNT` (10) — only keywords shared by at most 10 library movies qualify, so a broad keyword like "based on a novel" can't produce a boring, obvious group. Director/genre/decade/cast categories are unchanged. **Deliberately not implemented**: splitting cast into separate Actor/Actress categories — TMDB `gender` is stripped from the stored `movie.cast` at save time (`AddRating.js` only keeps `{name, character}`), so this data isn't available synchronously the way Favorites' actor/actress split gets it (an async per-person TMDB details fetch + cache, `favoriteTuning.js`'s pattern) — would need the same machinery grafted onto puzzle generation, a bigger change than a category swap.
  - **Unlimited guesses, no loss state (Jul 2026, bug report — "it's still really hard so... get rid of the limited number of guesses")**: the old 4-mistake cap (`MAX_MISTAKES`) and its `status === 'lost'` branch are gone entirely — `status` is now just `'playing'` or `'won'`, matching Reel Wordle's earlier "unlimited tries" precedent. The old "loss reveals every category" fallback is gone too (nothing to reveal early; you just keep guessing). Subtitle changed from "N mistakes remaining" to "N mistakes so far," same framing Wordle uses for its guess count.
  - **Wrong guesses label WHICH tiles overlap, not just how many (Jul 2026, same bug report)**: previously `lastGuessFeedback` only said "2 of those are in the same group" (a bare count). Now `submitGuess` finds the SPECIFIC category with the largest overlap (not just its size) and assigns it a stable 1-4 `categoryHintNumbers[label]` the first time it's discovered (lazily, in discovery order — never reassigned once set, so a later guess that re-touches the same category, e.g. revealing a 3rd/4th member, extends the same number rather than creating a new one). The actual overlapping tile keys get denormalized into `tileHints[key] = { number, color }` (color = that category's real difficulty tier, same legend `.category-legend` already shows) so the template needs no reverse lookup. Tiles carry a small colored circular `.tile-hint-badge` (number, on the difficulty color) in the top-right corner of their poster for as long as they're unsolved — persists across guesses (only cleared by `start()`), so a player can build up "these two go together" knowledge across multiple wrong attempts, per the report's own framing ("I could group those two together, [then] figure out what else goes with them").
  - **Solved groups render as poster rows, not a vanishing text summary (Jul 2026, bug report — "the posters just seemed to vanish... align them at the top with an outline... add another row each time")**: `solvedCategories` used to expose `titles` (a comma-joined string); it now exposes `tiles` (`{ key, entry }` pairs, re-derived from `puzzle.tiles` by key) so `.solved-group` can render actual poster `<img>`s in the same 4-column grid shape as the still-in-play `.tile-grid` below, bordered in the category's difficulty color (`:style="{ borderColor: category.difficulty?.color }"`, inline since it's per-instance data, not a fixed class). Each newly-solved category is simply another `v-for` row — no explicit "add a row" logic needed, that falls out of `solvedLabels` growing and Vue re-rendering the list.
  - Tests: `ConnectionsGame.test.js` — the old "reaching 4 mistakes" test replaced with a no-limit test (10 wrong guesses in a row, still `'playing'`, still solvable afterward); new tests for hint-number assignment/reuse across guesses, the tile badge count/text, solved-group poster count, and multiple solved rows staying visible simultaneously.
  - **Difficulty tiers (Jul 2026, bug report — "otherwise they're all too hard and it's impossible")**: each candidate category is now tagged by KIND (`decade`/`genre`/`director`/`cast`/`keyword`) with an editorial NYT-Connections-style difficulty tier via `DIFFICULTY_BY_KIND` — decade=yellow (tier 1, most surface-level), genre=green (2), director/cast=blue (3), keyword=purple (4, already the nichest kind per the `KEYWORD_MAX_MOVIE_COUNT` curation above). `generateConnectionsPuzzle` now picks in two passes: pass 1 tries one category per tier (1→4) before falling back to any remaining candidate in pass 2 — so a puzzle naturally spans a range of difficulty instead of clustering on whichever type happened to shuffle first. Each returned category carries `.difficulty` (`{tier, name, color}`); solved groups render with that color as their border + label (`ConnectionsGame.vue`'s `.solved-group`, inline `:style`, not baked into the scoped CSS class since it's per-instance data).
  - **Category-kind legend + decluttered tiles (Jul 2026, bug report)**: tiles no longer show a `.tile-title` caption below the poster (the poster art already carries the title — was pure redundancy). In its place, a static `.category-legend` line lists the 5 possible category KINDS (`CATEGORY_KIND_LABELS`, color-matched to the difficulty tiers above) up front — deliberately generic/always-the-same-5, not the actual 4 categories in the current puzzle, so it orients the player ("look for shared director/genre/decade/cast/keyword") without spoiling the answer.
  - **Wrong-guess partial-credit feedback (Jul 2026, bug report — "tell me if I had any number of that group correct")**: on a wrong guess, `submitGuess` computes the largest overlap between the 4 selected tiles and any single category (the same signal real Connections' "One away!" is based on, generalized to any count) and shows it via `lastGuessFeedback`/`.guess-feedback` ("2 of those are in the same group." / "Not even a pair in there…"). Always-rendered with a reserved `min-height` (`visibility: hidden` when empty) so the message appearing doesn't reflow the Deselect/Submit buttons below it — same reflow-avoidance pattern as Higher/Lower's status line above. Cleared on the next `toggleTile` so it doesn't linger past the guess it was about.
  - **Progress persistence, added Jul 2026 (bug report — "I had like one match going and then today I opened it... it was gone and it reset")**: Connections had NO persistence at all, unlike every other game — the only one of the 7 that reset on every mount. Follows the same `localStorage`-key pattern as Reel Wordle/Six Degrees/Timeline, but with a real structural difference: a puzzle's random generation (which 4 categories, which 16 tiles, in what shuffled order) can't be reproduced from a compact seed the way Reel Wordle's single target or Six Degrees' source/target pair can. So `persistState()` (`cinemaRoll.connections.current`) stores the actual tile ORDER as entry keys plus the category structure (`{label, keys, difficulty}`, already plain/JSON-safe) directly, rather than a seed — `tryRestore()` re-resolves each tile's real `entry` object from the CURRENT `eligibleGameEntries` by key (same "persist identity, re-derive data" principle the other games use), bailing to a fresh `start()` if any tile's movie is no longer eligible (unrated/removed since) or the puzzle was already WON (same "don't resume a finished round" decision already made for Reel Wordle/Six Degrees). `start()` and `submitGuess()` both call `persistState()` — `start()`'s call means "New Puzzle" always overwrites whatever was saved.
    - **Found while adding this, not separately reported: the same empty-graph-race class of bug Six Degrees had.** `created(){ this.start(); }` built the puzzle synchronously against whatever `eligibleGameEntries` held AT THAT INSTANT — on a fresh/direct navigation before the library finishes loading, that's an empty array, and nothing ever retried once real data arrived, permanently stranding on "not enough movies." Fixed the identical way Six Degrees was: `created()` now only handles the banner swap; puzzle loading moved to a `watch: { eligibleGameEntries: { immediate: true, handler(entries) { if (this.puzzle || !entries.length) return; this.loadOrStart(); } } }`.
    - **Test-file gotcha, also shared with Reel Wordle/Six Degrees**: `ConnectionsGame.test.js`'s fixtures (`buildSolvableLibrary()`) return the same dbKeys on every call, so persisted state from one test was leaking into and changing the outcome of the NEXT test in the same file (six pre-existing tests started failing the moment persistence landed, purely from stale localStorage — not real regressions). Fixed with the same `beforeEach(() => window.localStorage.clear())` guard those other two test files already use.
    - Tests: a new persistence describe block — restores a solved category + mistakes + hints across a remount, does NOT resume a won puzzle, "New Puzzle" overwrites stale saved progress, falls back to a fresh puzzle when a saved tile's movie is no longer eligible, and the empty-`eligibleGameEntries`-then-populated recovery case (mirroring Six Degrees' own regression test, `getters` wrapped in Vue's `reactive()` so the mock is actually trackable).
  - **Awards category, added Jul 2026 (user idea — "movies that have all won the best actor or best picture... Best sound designer any of the Academy Awards or really any of the awards")**: a new `awards` category kind (tier 3/blue, alongside director/cast/title) groups movies by which award CATEGORY they won (e.g. "Won Best Picture (Academy)", "Won Best Director (Personal)"), wins only (nominations excluded). Went through two rounds:
    - **v1 (same day) — scoped to Best Picture only, and merged personal+Academy into one shared label.** At the time, the only Academy data cached anywhere was `state.academyAwardWinners.bestPicture` (see below) — every other Academy category lived only behind a live per-movie fetch, too expensive to call once per eligible library movie at puzzle-generation time. A movie winning Best Picture via EITHER personal or Academy awards was folded into one shared `'Best Picture'` label.
    - **v2 (immediate follow-up — "are you saying you smushed together my personal awards and the Academy Awards? Best picture? I don't want that. They should be separate. So only match movies within each award")**: the merge was reverted. `movieWonAwardCategories` now suffixes every category name with its source — `` `${name} (Academy)` `` / `` `${name} (Personal)` `` — so a movie that won Best Picture in BOTH systems now produces TWO independent candidate categories that can never share a tile, rather than one combined pool. This unblocked the deeper fix below in the same round, since the user ALSO said (same message): "there aren't that many Academy Awards. It'd be like one JSON thing we could pull down from that service... store it locally so that we can use it wherever we want to" — see the new **Full Academy Awards Dataset, Cached Locally** section below. With the full dataset now available, the awards category covers ALL 32 real Academy categories, not just Best Picture.
    - **Personal awards side (unchanged across both rounds)** — fully synchronous, no fetch: `settings.personalAwards[year].categories[key].winner.movieId` (a TMDB id) checked against every entry across every saved year, for all 13 `PERSONAL_AWARD_CATEGORIES`.
    - **Academy side, now the full dataset**: `buildAcademyWinsByTmdbId(allAcademyAwards)` builds a `{ tmdbId(string) -> Set<categoryName> }` lookup ONCE per `buildCandidateCategories` call (not per-entry — the raw dataset is ~11k records, too many to re-scan per library movie) from `state.allAcademyAwards`. **Keyed by STRING id, not `Number(record.tmdb)`** — the API's `tmdb` field is a numeric-looking string, and comparing as strings (`String(record.tmdb)` vs `String(entry.movie.id)`) avoids any `Number()`-coercion edge case; this was caught directly by a test using non-numeric fixture ids (`'bp-0'` etc.), which is exactly the kind of ID shape mismatch a purely-numeric comparison could also silently break on with real-but-oddly-typed data.
    - **`connectionsGenerator.js`'s pure-module contract widened, not broken**: `buildCandidateCategories(eligibleEntries, awardsData)` / `generateConnectionsPuzzle(eligibleEntries, rng, awardsData)` both gained an optional parameter — omitting it (every pre-existing test call site) produces zero awards candidates rather than throwing. `ConnectionsGame.vue`'s `awardsData` computed pre-extracts `{ personalAwards, allAcademyAwards }` from the store.
    - **Tolerates missing data as a soft degrade, not a blocker**: `settings.personalAwards` and `state.allAcademyAwards` both load asynchronously and may still be empty on a cold direct navigation straight to `/games/connections`. Unlike `eligibleGameEntries` (which the existing watcher blocks/retries on), awards data isn't watched or waited for — if it's not loaded yet, the awards category simply isn't a candidate for THAT one generated puzzle.
    - Tests: `connectionsGenerator.test.js` — a Best Picture category from the full Academy dataset, a NON-Best-Picture Academy category (Best Director) now working, Academy nominations excluded, a personal-award category, personal nominations excluded, a movie winning both systems producing two genuinely separate category objects (not one merged/double-counted), and a movie winning only one system never bleeding into the other's group. `ConnectionsGame.test.js` — the component derives `awardsData.allAcademyAwards` from the store correctly, personal-only wins never produce an Academy-labeled category, the graceful no-data case, and "Awards" in the legend. `CATEGORY_KIND_LABELS` bumped 6 → 7.

### Full Academy Awards Dataset, Cached Locally (Jul 2026)
User's own framing, from the same follow-up that asked for the awards category to keep personal/Academy separate: "there aren't that many Academy Awards. It'd be like one JSON thing we could pull down from that service that we actually... I've actually built, but where we get the award data, I think you should just go ahead and pull it down and store it locally so that we can use it wherever we want to. It's not that big, I don't think."

- **Verified live before building anything**: `curl`'d the bare `GET /awards` endpoint (no `category` query param) on the user's own self-built `film-awards-api` service (`web-production-b8145.up.railway.app`) — confirmed it returns the ENTIRE dataset: 10,883 records, 32 distinct categories, every ceremony since the 1st Academy Awards (1928), ~5.5MB raw JSON, ~2,384 of those records are actual wins. Nothing in the codebase referenced this bare endpoint before — the only two existing call sites were `?category=Best%20Picture` (store) and `/awards/tmdb/{movieId}` (per-movie, `MovieDetail.vue`/`DBGridLayoutSearchResult.vue`) — so this was confirmed empirically, not assumed.
- **New Vuex state: `state.allAcademyAwards`** (array, default `[]`) — the full raw dataset, normalized the same way the existing Best-Picture fetch already does (`isWinner`/`isActing` coerced from the API's `'TRUE'`/`'1'`/`true`-ish values to real booleans). **Deliberately separate from `state.academyAwardWinners`** (kept as-is, unchanged) rather than replacing it — `academyAwardWinners.bestPicture` holds FULL TMDB-enriched movie objects (poster, title, release date — needed by `Home.vue`'s "Best Picture" quick-link feature to show winners the user HASN'T rated yet) via a per-winner TMDB detail fetch; doing that same enrichment for all ~2,384 winners (let alone all ~11k records) would be a much larger, slower undertaking than "pull it down and store it locally" calls for. `allAcademyAwards` stays raw/unenriched — just `tmdb`/`category`/`year`/`isWinner`/`ceremony`/etc. — which is all any *new* synchronous consumer (like the Connections awards category) actually needs.
- **Fetched once per session inside `initializeDB`**, same structural convention as every other guarded fetch there (`Boolean(...).length` / `.length > 0` guard, `try/catch` + `console.error`+`ErrorLogService.error`, commit at the end) — but ALSO backed by the same IndexedDB snapshot race `movieLog`/`settings` already use (`loadSnapshot`/`saveSnapshot` from `src/utils/offlineStore.js`), specifically so the ~5.5MB payload doesn't have to be redownloaded from scratch on every single session once a device has fetched it once. Keyed by a **fixed** cache key (`'global'`, not the per-account `topKey`) since this data isn't user-specific — confirmed by NOT resetting it in `resetLocalDB` (unlike `movieLog`/`settings`/`academyAwardWinners`, which all get cleared there before switching/reloading an account).
- **Closes a documented offline gap for free**: an earlier CLAUDE.md note (under "Offline / PWA Support") explicitly flagged `academyAwardWinners`'s fetch as OUT of scope for the app's offline-support work. `allAcademyAwards` doesn't have that gap — it gets the exact same IndexedDB fallback movieLog/settings already rely on for a cold offline start.
- **Follow-up, same day ("go ahead and fix those things while you're there")**: `MovieDetail.vue` and `DBGridLayoutSearchResult.vue` migrated off their live per-movie `/awards/tmdb/{id}` fetches to read `state.allAcademyAwards` directly instead.
  - **Both now compute a per-result award list synchronously** (`awardsForMovie`/`awardsForResult`, same shape: `filter((award) => String(award.tmdb) === String(movieId))`), with `academyAwardWins`/`academyAwardNominations` just partitioning that by `isWinner` — no fetch, no loading state, no manual reset needed.
  - **`MovieDetail.vue`**: removed `data().awardsData`, the `getAwardsData()` method, and the `loadMovieData` fetch call — along with the explicit `this.awardsData = null;` reset that a documented bug (stale awards data leaking across a same-instance movie-to-movie navigation) had needed. That reset is now unnecessary, not just redundant: a plain computed derived from `this.movie` has nothing to go stale, so the entire bug CLASS is gone, not just this one instance of it. The regression test that used to exercise that reset (`MovieDetailAwards.test.js`) was rewritten to assert the same "switching movies shows the right movie's data" behavior against the new computed instead of the old fetch-mock.
  - **`DBGridLayoutSearchResult.vue`**: same migration, but here it ALSO fixes a real, independently-discovered bug — `showDetailsModal`'s watcher used to do `this.awardsData = this.getAwardsData();`, assigning the UN-AWAITED PROMISE (`getAwardsData` is `async`) rather than the resolved array. `Array.isArray(this.awardsData)` was therefore always `false`, so this component's whole Academy Awards section had been silently rendering empty for every user, every time, with no error — the kind of bug that's easy to ship and never notice because nothing throws. Fixed for free by the same computed-based redesign (there's no async assignment left to get wrong). First-ever dedicated test file for this component (`DBGridLayoutSearchResult.test.js`, `shallowMount` + a stubbed `v-lazy` directive) — narrowly scoped to the awards computeds specifically, not a broader coverage pass on a previously fully-untested, fairly large component.
  - Tests: `MovieDetailAwards.test.js`'s rewritten describe block (per-movie filtering from a shared dataset + a missing-data tolerance case). `DBGridLayoutSearchResult.test.js` (new — per-result filtering, the "used to silently render empty" case now surfacing real data, missing-dataset tolerance).
- Tests: `OfflineDataFallback.test.js` (imports the real `store/index.js`, same established pattern as its `movieLog`/`settings` tests) — fetches the bare `/awards` endpoint and normalizes booleans, falls back to the IndexedDB snapshot without live data clobbering it, the fetch-once guard (doesn't re-fetch once populated), and NOT being reset by `resetLocalDB`.
- **Six Degrees** (`sixDegrees.js` + `SixDegreesGame.vue`) — picks two movies connected through the library's shared-cast graph (`buildCastGraph`, capped at the 10 most-billed cast per movie — using the full cast would make nearly every movie "1 hop" from nearly every other one, which isn't an interesting puzzle) via BFS `shortestPath`, preferring a 2–4 hop gap (`pickConnectedPair`). The player then builds the chain themselves: type a cast member of the current movie, then a movie that person was also in, alternating, until reaching the target — not just watching a computed answer. "Reveal shortest path" shows the BFS-optimal chain as a fallback. **Persistence added (Jul 2026, bug report)**: progress used to reset on every mount (`created(){ this.start(); }` unconditionally picked a fresh random pair), so leaving the page — e.g. to poke around Home for inspiration — and coming back silently discarded the in-progress chain. Now follows Reel Wordle's exact pattern: `localStorage` key `cinemaRoll.sixDegrees.current` stores `{ sourceKey, targetKey, chain, revealed }`; `loadOrStart()`/`tryRestore()` looks it up on `created()`, re-finds both endpoint movies in `eligibleGameEntries`, and **recomputes** `optimalPath`/`optimalHops` via `shortestPath(graph, sourceKey, targetKey)` rather than persisting them (they're deterministically derivable from the graph, so no need to store BFS output). Falls back to a fresh `start()` if either endpoint is missing (movie unrated/filtered since) or the path can no longer be found. `pick()`, `revealPath()`, and `start()` all call `persistState()` — `start()`'s call means "New Pair" always overwrites whatever was saved, same as Reel Wordle's "New Puzzle".
  - **Found while testing the persistence fix: a pre-existing empty-graph race, now also fixed.** `created(){ this.loadOrStart(); }` called `buildCastGraph`/`pickConnectedPair` synchronously — if `eligibleGameEntries` was still empty (library not yet loaded from Firebase, most visible on a direct/deep-link navigation straight to `/games/six-degrees` before `dbLoaded` settles) this silently built an empty graph, `pair` stayed `null` forever, and the page was permanently stuck on "not enough movies" even after the real library arrived — nothing ever retried. Same class of bug Reel Wordle already guards against. Fixed the same way: `created()` replaced with a `watch: { eligibleGameEntries: { immediate: true, handler(entries) { if (this.pair || !entries.length) return; this.loadOrStart(); } } }`, so it retries once real data lands instead of only ever running once at the wrong moment. Regression test mounts with an empty `getters.allMediaAsArray` (wrapped in Vue's `reactive()` so the mock is actually trackable), asserts `pair` is null, then populates it and asserts `pair`/`graph` recover.
  - **Two separate cast graphs, capped vs. full (Jul 2026, bug report — "it doesn't seem to be able to find the movies I'm looking for")**: the single `graph` (capped at 10 most-billed cast per movie, per `buildCastGraph`'s own default) was being used for BOTH puzzle generation AND the in-play autocomplete. That cap is deliberately there so `pickConnectedPair` doesn't make almost every movie "1 hop" from almost every other one — but reusing it for suggestions meant a real, correct connection through a supporting actor billed 11th+ was invisible to the graph and silently produced zero suggestions, reading as broken autocomplete. Now builds a SECOND graph, `playGraph` (`buildCastGraph(eligibleGameEntries, Infinity)` — full cast, no cap), and `onInput`'s suggestions (both the "who was in that movie" and "what else were they in" directions) read from `playGraph` instead of `graph`. `graph` (capped) is still used for `pickConnectedPair`/`shortestPath` — puzzle generation and "reveal shortest path" are unaffected, since letting the PLAYER use the full cast doesn't make solving easier (they still have to know/recall the connection themselves), it only removes an arbitrary billing-order dead end. Regression test: an 11th-billed actor connecting two movies is invisible to `graph.peopleByMovie` but surfaces correctly via `onInput` (which now reads `playGraph`).
  - **Visual redesign, round 2 (Jul 2026, bug reports on the just-shipped chain-row redesign)**: the 4 separate pill buttons ("New Game"/"New Easy Game"/etc.) became ONE connected segmented control (`.difficulty-segments`, a `New game:` label + Easy/Medium/Hard segments in a single pill-shaped border) — "Any" was dropped from the UI entirely (`difficultyOptions` now just maps `DIFFICULTY_LEVELS`), though `selectedDifficulty === null` (unconstrained) is still the internal default before the player's first tap, and the not-enough-movies gate's recovery picker still works the same way (if the broadest/unconstrained search found nothing, no narrower tier can either, so it's only shown once a specific tier has already been tried and failed). Chain-steps unified to one shared width (movie and person alike, 104px, up from the mixed 118/84px) and the page's horizontal padding was trimmed (1rem → 0.5rem) so 3 steps comfortably fit across a real phone viewport — "still larger than the original 100px" and "3 across using more of the width" pull in different directions on their own, resolved by anchoring "larger" to the pre-redesign baseline and "smaller" to the immediately-prior 118px iteration. The target/goal marker's dimmed-opacity + dashed-border treatment was removed outright (`.chain-step.goal` no longer overrides anything visually — feedback was simply "didn't need it"). Every chain-step (`<button>` now, not `<div>`, matching ConnectionsGame's tile pattern) is tappable — `goToChainItem(item)` pushes `/movie/:id` for a movie step or calls `searchForPerson(name)` (the same store-commit sequence as `MovieDetail.searchFor(name, 'cast')`, replicated locally rather than shared) for a person step; both apply to the in-progress chain row AND the post-reveal optimal-path row. A small circular `.chain-delete-badge` (`@click.stop`, so it doesn't also fire the step's own navigate handler) appears only on entries the player actually added (index > 0, excluding the synthetic goal marker, and only while `status === 'playing'`) — `deleteFromChain(index)` truncates `this.chain` back to (not including) that index and re-persists, so a remount resumes the trimmed chain. `start()` now scrolls to the top (`window.scrollTo({top:0, behavior:'instant'})`, GamesHub's own convention) before rebuilding the puzzle, covering both the difficulty segments and "New Pair" (both funnel through `start()`).
  - **Per-game icons, shared source of truth (Jul 2026, same round)**: `GAME_ICONS` (route path → `bi-*` class) now lives in `gameData.js` alongside `LAST_PLAYED_KEY` — `GamesHub.vue`'s tile array and `Home.vue`'s Games entry-point button both read from it instead of each hardcoding the same 4 strings. `Home.vue` gained a `gamesButtonIcon` computed (mirrors `goToGames()`'s own `LAST_PLAYED_KEY` lookup) so the button shows the SPECIFIC game's icon when `goToGames()` would jump straight back into it, falling back to a generic `bi-dice-5-fill` icon (changed from `bi-controller` per direct feedback) when it would land on the hub instead. A plain (non-reactive-to-localStorage) computed is sufficient here — the only way `LAST_PLAYED_KEY` changes is by visiting a game and returning, which necessarily remounts `Home.vue` anyway, so a fresh read on each mount is already "fresh enough."

Tests: `src/test/games/SixDegreesGame.test.js` grew a `deleting a chain entry`/`clicking a chain item navigates`/`starting a new game scrolls to the top` set alongside the rewritten difficulty-picker suite (segment text, `.active` class, "Easy" as the sparse-fixture recovery pick since "Any" is gone from the UI).
  - **Visual redesign, round 3 (Jul 2026, bug reports on round 2)**: `.chain-row`'s `align-items` had been changed to `flex-start` in round 2 for the 3-across layout work, which silently regressed the person-photo/movie-poster vertical centering the player specifically liked — reverted to `align-items: center` (the 3-across fit is controlled by width/padding, not cross-axis alignment, so nothing else needed to change). The difficulty segmented control is now full-width (`.difficulty-picker { width: 100% }`, each `.difficulty-segment { flex: 1 }`) and color-coded per tier via a small SCSS `$difficulty-tier-colors` map + `@each` loop bound through a `[level.key, {active}]` class binding — outlined (colored border + text, transparent fill) by default, solid-filled (colored bg, white text) once selected. Colors are a traffic-light-style green/orange/red (easy/medium/hard), independent of the app's existing amber accent and of Connections' unrelated difficulty-tier palette (different game, different meaning for "tier").
  - **Per-game header banner, THREE attempts, landed on a movie-title pun (Jul 2026)**: the shared `Header.vue` stays mounted and visible on every game screen (see the BackLink section above) and is a pure renderer of `store.state.bannerUrl` — left alone, that just means a game screen keeps showing whatever movie backdrop Home last set, unrelated to the game. `SixDegreesGame.vue` swaps it on `created()` (saving the current `bannerUrl` first as `this.previousBannerUrl`) and restores that saved value (or `null`) on `beforeUnmount()`. Both store calls are optional-chained (`$store.commit?.(...)`, `$store.state?.bannerUrl`) since several game test mocks stub `$store` without those - same defensive convention `gameData.js` already established. **This save/restore shell is the only part that survived all three attempts** — what gets swapped IN changed twice:
    1. *v1*: a hand-drawn 16:9 SVG - "SIX DEGREES" wordmark + a row of alternating movie-poster/person-photo icons, laid out around the BackLink/Cinema-Roll-title safe zones.
    2. *v2 (immediate feedback — "I don't love the graphic. I don't like the font; it competes with the app title... I do like the constellations in the background. Maybe lean into that?")*: dropped the wordmark and literal icons for a pure constellation/network graphic - full-bleed starfield plus a glowing 6-node hub path.
    3. *v3, landed (immediate follow-up — "let's lose the graphic idea... we could use movies that have titles like the game we're doing... Six Degrees of Separation")*: no generated art at all. `SixDegreesGame.vue` now sets `bannerUrl` to a **hardcoded TMDB backdrop URL** for the real 1993 film *Six Degrees of Separation* (TMDB id 23210, backdrop `/e9L5dpi41k3YkbavwgOGXnQGCe1.jpg`, found via `/search/movie` and eyeballed before wiring in) - `https://image.tmdb.org/t/p/w500/...`, same `w500` sizing Home.vue's own banner uses. `src/assets/images/games/six-degrees-banner.svg` and its `import` are deleted; the constant is named `THEMED_BANNER_URL` for whenever a second game adopts the same pun-title pattern (not yet extracted to a shared map/mixin - only one caller so far, same "extract on the second caller" call as the save/restore shell itself).
    - Tests: `SixDegreesGame.test.js`'s themed-banner describe block asserts the exact TMDB URL substring (`image.tmdb.org` + the backdrop's own id) rather than a generic string, so it'll actually fail if the hardcoded id/path ever gets fat-fingered.
  - **Difficulty segments toned down from a full pill to a subtle 8px rounded rect (Jul 2026, bug report — "I don't like how rounded they are... much less oval and much more rectangular")**: `.difficulty-segments`' `border-radius` and the matching `:first-child`/`:last-child` corner overrides all dropped from `999px` to `8px`. Scoped to just this one control (not the shared `.btn-game` pill buttons elsewhere in Games, which weren't what was screenshotted/discussed).
  - **Difficulty segments shrunk again, immediate follow-up (Jul 2026, bug report — "too poofy... I like them smaller")**: after going full-width (round 3), `.difficulty-segment`'s padding/font-size had crept up from the original compact pill-button values. Reduced to `padding: 0.25rem 0.3rem` / `font-size: 0.75rem` (from `0.45rem 0.4rem` / `0.85rem`) - still full-width and flex:1 (that part wasn't what was flagged), just visually tighter.
  - **`<h1 class="game-title">Six Degrees</h1>` removed (Jul 2026, bug report — "get rid of the title of the game and instead just have the description of how you play")**: the page now opens straight into `.game-subtitle` ("Connect these two through shared cast members...") right after `BackLink`, which picked up the h1's old `margin: 0.5rem 0 0.25rem` top spacing (`margin-top: 0.5rem` added to `.game-subtitle`) so removing the heading didn't also collapse the gap under BackLink. The `not-enough-movies` gate never had its own separate title to begin with (it already opened directly with its own message paragraph), so it's unaffected.
  - **Four more fixes in the same round (Jul 2026)**:
    - **More horizontal padding, without breaking the 3-across chain fit** ("the description... doesn't have enough horizontal padding from the edges"): `.six-degrees-game`'s padding went back from `0.5rem` to `1rem`, and `.chain-row` picked up a matching `margin: 0 -0.5rem` to claw back exactly the difference - net effective inset for the chain-row is unchanged (still fits 3 across), while everything else (subtitle, the new-game panel, etc.) gets the full `1rem`.
    - **Running hop counter dropped from the description** ("the count of how many hops I've taken so far is unnecessary. Lose it."): `.game-subtitle` is now the static "Connect these two through shared cast members." with no `{{ hopsSoFar }}` interpolation. `hopsSoFar` itself is untouched (still computed, still used in the post-win "Connected in N hops" summary - that's a final score, not a running counter, and wasn't what was flagged).
    - **Difficulty picker + "New Game" moved to the bottom, and split into a pure picker + a separate start action** ("the new game buttons still look sort of bad. Let's move... to the bottom. Let's change it from three buttons to one new game button but with a switch... full width switch, full width button"). Previously each of the 3 segments both selected AND immediately started a fresh pair. Now `selectDifficulty(level)` just sets `selectedDifficulty` (no `start()` call); a single full-width `.btn-game-primary` "New Game" button underneath actually calls `start()`, reading whatever tier is currently selected. One `.new-game-panel` (the switch + the button - originally had a "Difficulty" label above it, dropped a round later, see below) is rendered ONCE per view - at the bottom of the main play view (after the chain row and the guess-form/result-banner, covering every status: playing, won, revealed) and in the `not-enough-movies` gate (now always shown there, not just when a specific tier had already been tried - a retry can still succeed by chance since `pickConnectedPair`'s search is randomized). The old separate "New Pair" button in the win/reveal result-banner is gone, superseded by this one persistent control.
    - **Guess prompt now names the actual movie/person instead of generic phrasing** ("Who was in Rebel Without A Cause?" / "what else was Rock Hudson in?"): new `guessPlaceholder` computed reads the chain's last entry - `Who was in ${title}?` when a person is needed, `What else was ${name} in?` when a movie is needed - falling back to the original generic phrasing only if the last entry somehow isn't resolved.
  - **Round 5 (Jul 2026, same day): symmetric description spacing, a "Give me one" hint, input repositioned, label dropped**:
    - **`.game-subtitle`'s margin-top/margin-bottom equalized** ("make the space above and below the game description match") - both `0.75rem` now (was `0.5rem`/`1rem`).
    - **The single "Reveal shortest path" button is now a two-segment "Give me one" / "Give up" control** (`.hint-actions`/`.hint-segment`, same visual language as the difficulty switch but with no persistent active state - both are one-tap actions). "Give up" is the unchanged `revealPath()` behavior (shows the whole remaining path, ends the round) with a red-tinted border (`.give-up`, matching Hard's color) since it's the more final choice. "Give me one" is new: `nextHintStep(lastLink, usedMovieKeys, graph, playGraph, targetKey)` in `sixDegrees.js` recomputes a FRESH shortest path from the chain's CURRENT last entry (not the original `optimalPath`) - important because the player may have already taken a different, still-valid route than the one the puzzle was generated from, so replaying the stale optimalPath could hand back a step that no longer connects to where they actually are. From a movie, it's just `shortestPath(graph, ...)`'s first person. From a person, it evaluates every unused candidate movie that person was in (via the UNCAPPED `playGraph`, same "don't let the puzzle-generation billing cap limit what a player can find" reasoning as the existing autocomplete) and picks whichever gets closest to the target per the CAPPED `graph`'s own shortest-path distance. Returns `null` (silent no-op) if already at the target or genuinely stuck. The component's `giveHint()` method feeds the result through the existing `pick()` method, so a hint updates the chain/photo-cache/persistence exactly like a real guess would.
    - **Guess input moved above the chain-row images, hint actions stayed below** ("moving the input above the images and leave the give up buttons below"). `.guess-form` now holds only the input + suggestions dropdown (the old "Reveal shortest path" button that used to live inside it is gone, replaced by the separate `.hint-actions` row after the chain-row).
    - **"Difficulty" label removed** ("we can lose the difficulty label") - `.new-game-panel` no longer has a label above the switch; the "New Game" button right below already makes its purpose clear on its own.
  - **Round 6 (Jul 2026, same day): less busy, and a scrolling chain**:
    - **`.chain-row` gained `margin-top: 1rem`** ("we need some space below the input above the posters").
    - **New-game controls now HIDDEN while playing, swapping in for the hint actions once the round ends** ("too many buttons... hide the new game buttons until someone clicks give up... swap the help/give up buttons with the difficulty and new game buttons"). `.hint-actions` (`v-if="status === 'playing'"`) and the `.result-banner`+`.new-game-panel` pair (`v-else`) now occupy the SAME slot in the template, mutually exclusive by status - previously `.new-game-panel` was unconditionally rendered at the bottom regardless of status, doubling up with `.hint-actions` while a round was still in progress.
    - **`.new-game-panel` reverted from "picker + separate New Game button" back to "label + auto-starting segments"** ("let's go back to having 'new game' as a label and then three buttons for difficulty"). `selectDifficulty(level)` now sets `selectedDifficulty` AND calls `start()` in one step again (undoing the decoupling from two rounds prior); the standalone full-width "New Game" `.btn-game-primary` button is gone. The panel is a `New game:` label + `.difficulty-segments` in one flex row (structurally back to the round-3 shape), reused as-is in both the main view (now only reachable once `status !== 'playing'`, per the bullet above) and the `not-enough-movies` gate (always shown there, unchanged). Rationale for reverting the two-step flow: now that this panel only appears once a round has already ended, a single tap to both choose a tier and start is simpler than picking-then-confirming.
    - **The chain row scrolls horizontally instead of wrapping to multiple lines** ("let's have them all appear in one horizontally scrolling list... scroll back and forth with your finger to see the whole chain"). `.chain-row`: `flex-wrap: nowrap` + `overflow-x: auto` (+ `-webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch` for iOS momentum), `justify-content` changed from `center` to `flex-start` (centered content that later overflows a scroll container can leave its own start unreachable in some browsers), and `.chain-step`/`.chain-arrow` both got `flex-shrink: 0` so items keep their fixed size instead of being squeezed to fit. The chain only grows longer as a round progresses, so wrapping meant the page kept getting taller - this keeps a constant height. The existing `margin: 0 -0.5rem` "claw back the container padding" trick is kept and paired with a NEW `padding: 0 0.5rem` directly on `.chain-row` itself, so the scrollable area extends to the screen edges (more room to scroll into) while the resting (`scrollLeft: 0`) position of the first item still lines up with the rest of the page's normal inset, same as before.
  - Tests: the `"New game:" difficulty picker` describe block in `SixDegreesGame.test.js` was rewritten around the reverted auto-start behavior and the hidden-while-playing gating (calls `revealPath()` first to reach a non-playing status before asserting the panel exists). The horizontal-scroll CSS change needed no test changes (nothing was asserting on wrap/scroll behavior).
  - **Round 7 (Jul 2026, same day): "?" placeholder slots, and give-up replaces rather than duplicates**:
    - **Placeholder slots fill the gap between the built chain and the target** ("add a question mark entry between the two movies when the game starts... treatments for both question mark people and question mark posters... fill in the gap between the last entry and the end appropriately"). `chainDisplayItems` now inserts placeholder items (`isPlaceholder: true`) between the real chain and the goal marker: exactly ONE person-shaped placeholder when a person is needed next, or a movie-shaped placeholder followed by a person-shaped placeholder when a movie is needed next - always ending on a person placeholder immediately before the goal, since the goal is itself the final movie. This isn't an attempt to predict the real remaining hop count (which could be longer); it's a fixed 1-or-2-slot skeleton that keeps the row visually consistent as you play. Rendered with a new `.chain-placeholder` class (dashed border, muted fill, big "?") layered on top of the existing `.chain-poster`/`.chain-photo` shape/size rules - a movie placeholder is `.chain-poster.chain-placeholder`, a person placeholder is `.chain-photo.chain-placeholder`. Placeholders are inert: `handleChainItemClick` early-returns for them (no navigation) and the delete badge's `v-if` excludes them (nothing to delete).
    - **Giving up now REPLACES the built chain row instead of showing a second one below it** ("when I give up, the shortest path should replace my built path not show both of them"). Previously `.chain-row` (the player's own progress, placeholders and all) was unconditionally rendered up top, and a SEPARATE `.chain-row.revealed-chain` rendered the true shortest path inside the result banner once revealed - both visible at once. New `displayChain` computed picks ONE source for the single remaining `.chain-row`: `revealedChain` (mapped with synthetic `itemKey`s) once `status === 'revealed'`, otherwise `chainDisplayItems` as before. The result banner now only ever holds its message text; the duplicate chain-row markup inside it (and the now-dead `.revealed-chain` CSS rule) is gone.
    - Tests: `SixDegreesGame.test.js` - two new placeholder-count assertions (person-only vs movie-then-person), a placeholder-is-inert test, and a give-up-replaces-not-duplicates test asserting exactly one `.chain-row` whose length matches `revealedChain`, not the built chain.
  - **Round 8 (Jul 2026, same day): labels trimmed, auto-connect to the target, win/reveal message moved to the top, and a custom banner graphic**:
    - **Labels dropped under posters and placeholders, kept under portraits** ("we don't need the labels under the posters and question marks... we do need the names under the portraits"). `.chain-step-label` is now `v-if="item.type === 'person' && !item.isPlaceholder"` - only real person entries get a name caption underneath; movie posters (real or "?") and person "?" placeholders don't.
    - **Picking a person who's also in the target auto-completes the connection** ("when I get a person who is in the final movie it should just make that final connection for me. I shouldn't have to type the target movie"). `pick()`'s person branch now checks whether the newly-added person is also connected to `pair.target` (via the uncapped `playGraph.moviesByPerson`, same "don't let the puzzle-generation billing cap limit what a player can find" reasoning used elsewhere) and, if so, appends the target movie automatically in the same call. `giveHint()` already routes through `pick()`, so a hinted person who happens to border the target now auto-completes too, for free. **Test-fixture fallout**: several existing tests picked a random cast member of a randomly-chosen `pair.source` and asserted the chain stayed at length 2 - now flaky-prone if that random pair happened to be a direct 1-hop connection. Added a `forceLongPair(wrapper, entries)` test helper (forces `buildConnectedLibrary`'s two endpoints, 5 hops apart, so no single actor can ever span both) and applied it to every test where an early auto-complete would break the assertion; left it off tests that don't care (e.g. ones that just look up a person by CSS class, which still resolves correctly either way).
    - **Win/reveal message moved to the top, replacing the description and input** ("'Connected in X hops (shortest possible: 2)' it looks bad. Let's instead put that message at the top in place of the input and the game description"). New `.result-message` paragraph sits exactly where `.game-subtitle` was (same margins), swapping in via `v-else` once `status !== 'playing'`; the old boxed `.result-banner` (colored background, padding) lower on the page is gone entirely - `.result-message` is plain text, colored green for `won` / muted gray for `revealed`, consistent with sitting where a plain description line used to be rather than looking like a separate alert box.
    - **Custom banner graphic + hidden "Cinema Roll" logo** (user-provided image, "It's in the downloads folder... Use this instead of the normal banner. We can lose the CinemaRoll logo too."). Replaces the earlier "Six Degrees of Separation" movie-backdrop pun (three banner attempts total now - see the round-6/7 history above). Source PNG was 2560×1440 (exactly 16:9) but 3.8MB - resized/re-encoded to a 1200×675 JPEG (`src/assets/images/games/six-degrees-banner.jpg`, ~260KB) since it only ever displays at mobile-banner width and the app has an established practice of trimming image payloads (see "Asset / Image Performance" above); visually indistinguishable at display size. New Vuex state `hideHeaderLogo` (+ `setHideHeaderLogo` mutation) - separate from `showHeader` (which hides the WHOLE header) - lets `Header.vue` conditionally hide just the `.home-link` "Cinema Roll" title overlay while keeping the banner itself visible, since this graphic has its own "SIX DEGREES / CINEMA ROLL GAMES" branding baked into its bottom-right corner and the two would otherwise overlap. `SixDegreesGame.vue` sets it `true` on `created()` alongside the banner swap, resets to `false` on `beforeUnmount()` (unconditional reset is safe - no other screen sets this flag `true` yet). Tests: `src/test/Header.test.js` (new file - logo visibility toggling, banner still renders independently of the logo).
  - **Round 9 (Jul 2026, same day): finished rounds aren't resumable, banner is tap-to-home again, outline dice icon**:
    - **A finished round (won or given up) is no longer restored on the next visit** ("you don't need to save my result... the next time I return to the game it should just be a new game"). `tryRestore()` now checks the persisted state right after parsing it: if `saved.revealed` is true, or the saved chain's last link is a movie matching `saved.targetKey` (i.e. it was won), `tryRestore()` returns `false` immediately instead of reconstructing that state - `loadOrStart()` then falls through to a fresh `start()`, same as if nothing had been saved at all. The stale finished entry in localStorage isn't explicitly cleared - the next `start()` overwrites it via the existing `persistState()` call, so it never lingers past one fresh game.
    - **The header banner is clickable-to-home again** ("clicking on the new banner should bring me to the home screen still"). Hiding the "Cinema Roll" title overlay (`hideHeaderLogo`, previous round) removed the only tap-to-home affordance the header had while that title was hidden. `Header.vue`'s `.random-banner` now also has `@click="goHome"` (the same method the title already used) - applies globally, not just on Six Degrees, since tapping a banner image to go home is a reasonable affordance everywhere and this was the simplest fix that didn't require Header.vue to know which specific game hid its logo.
    - **Games-button fallback icon switched from `bi-dice-5-fill` to the outline `bi-dice-5`** ("the die that isn't colored in") - one-line change in `Home.vue`'s `gamesButtonIcon` computed, not unit-tested (this specific icon string was never asserted on).
  - **Version number regression, fixed later (Jul 2026, bug report — "the version number that appears in the header doesn't appear with our new game headers... just the exact same spot... over the new banner image")**: `hideHeaderLogo` was gating the ENTIRE `.home-link` div, which held both the "Cinema Roll" title AND the version number span nested inside it — hiding the title as designed also silently hid the version on every game with a custom banner (all of them by this point). `Header.vue` now has a `v-else` sibling, `.version-only`, rendering just the version in the same bottom-right corner + dark-pill treatment `.home-link` already used, whenever `hideHeaderLogo` is true. Tests: `Header.test.js` gained a describe block (version shows inside `.home-link` normally, falls back to standalone `.version-only` when the logo's hidden, and `.version-only` still navigates home on click same as the title did).
    - Tests: `SixDegreesGame.test.js` gained two "does NOT resume" cases (won, revealed) under the persistence describe block; `Header.test.js` gained a banner-click-navigates-home case.
  - **Difficulty segmented-control corner bug (Jul 2026, bug report + screenshot)**: each `.difficulty-segment` carries its own full per-tier colored border (needed for the outline-vs-filled look), while the container clips to a pill shape via `border-radius: 999px; overflow: hidden`. Clipping a SQUARE-cornered bordered element against a ROUNDED boundary left a stray crescent of border poking out past the pill's silhouette at both ends. Fixed by giving `:first-child`/`:last-child` their own matching outer corner radii directly, so there's nothing square left for the clip to cut awkwardly.
- **Taste Quiz — built, then removed (Jul 2026)**: auto-generated multiple-choice trivia about aggregated stats. Removed per direct feedback ("isn't very fun... let's just lose it") after real use — the questions read as too obvious to be engaging. `TasteQuizGame.vue`, `quizGenerator.js`, their tests, and the `/games/quiz` route are gone; if revisited, the reason for the previous design (`topCountQuestion()` bailing rather than guessing when data can't support an unambiguous answer) is worth preserving even in a fresh attempt.
- **Custom button look, scoped to Games (Jul 2026, bug report — "a lot of the buttons on our site feel like they're just straight out of bootstrap")**: the report was site-wide and the user was explicit they didn't have a concrete direction ("I'm not sure exactly") — asked via `AskUserQuestion` rather than guessing at a full redesign, and the user chose a Games-only pass over a site-wide one, an example-first preview, or talking through direction first. New partial `src/assets/scss/_game-buttons.scss` defines `.btn-game` (base: pill shape, `:active`-only press feedback per the mobile-first/no-hover convention, real `:disabled` handling) plus `.btn-game-primary` (amber gradient pill, replaces `.btn-warning`) and `.btn-game-secondary` (bordered/transparent, replaces `.btn-outline-light`), with a `.btn-game-sm` size modifier. Imported via `@import '@/assets/scss/game-buttons';` into each of the four game components' own `<style lang="scss" scoped>` (Vue SFC + sass-loader scopes an `@import`ed partial's rules to that component exactly like inline rules, so nothing leaks) rather than duplicated by hand — this file is the single source of truth for the look; `src/assets/scss/` existed as an empty placeholder directory before this. **Deliberately NOT touched**: `GamesHub.vue`'s `.game-tile` cards and the autocomplete `.suggestion-item` buttons (already fully custom, not Bootstrap-derived, nothing to fix); `TweakInline.vue`'s `.btn-warning`/`.btn-outline-light` (same visual family, but outside the agreed Games-only scope — a natural next target if this direction lands well). Tests: `HigherLowerGame.test.js` selectors updated from `.btn-warning` to `.btn-game-primary` (the only tests that hardcoded the old Bootstrap class name).
  - **Round 2 (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up bug report — "still seems really bootstrap-y... more of the buttons [full width]... alerts... not look so bootstrap")**: the first pass only touched buttons; the biggest remaining offender was the plain `<input class="form-control">` in Reel Wordle/Six Degrees (Bootstrap's default white box, jarring against an otherwise all-dark UI) — new partial `src/assets/scss/_game-inputs.scss` (`.game-input`: dark background, subtle border, amber focus ring instead of Bootstrap's blue) replaces it in both. Standalone CTA buttons (Start/Play Again, both New Puzzle buttons, New Pair, Reveal shortest path) now use a `.full-width` utility (moved into `_game-buttons.scss` itself rather than duplicated per component) — `width:100%`, capped at `max-width:320px` for Higher/Lower's centered setup/game-over buttons specifically (`.cta-btn`) so it doesn't stretch edge-to-edge on a wide viewport; Connections' Deselect+Submit PAIR uses `flex:1` each instead (so together they fill the row, without literally going 100% each and stacking). The "alert-like" boxes (Wordle's `.target-clues` hint box and `.result-banner`, Connections' `.result-banner`) moved from a uniformly color-tinted box to a neutral dark background (`#1a1a1a`) with a 4px colored LEFT-BORDER accent — reads as a purpose-built callout rather than a generic Bootstrap-alert-shaped rectangle. `TweakInline.vue` is still out of scope (not touched).
- **Found while screenshotting the button pass, not user-reported: BackLink can overlap a game's own h1 title.** `BackLink`/`.home-shortcut` float at a fixed `(6,6)` of the viewport (the documented corner-lift trick — no positioned ancestor, escapes to the page's initial containing block) on the assumption the global Header above has real height to "lift over." At a genuine phone viewport (393×852, confirmed via `window.innerWidth`/`devicePixelRatio`), a fresh/direct navigation to a game route — before `Home.vue` had ever run and set `bannerUrl` — left `Header.vue`'s `.random-banner` with zero images and `height: 0`, so the h1 title (no top clearance of its own) rendered directly under the fixed-position back-link, overlapping it illegibly. Fix: all 4 game components + `GamesHub.vue` gained `padding-top: 2.5rem` on their root container — a fixed safety margin independent of the header's actual height, so the overlap can't happen regardless of whether a banner has been set yet. Purely additive CSS; when the header DOES have real height (the common case, navigating from Home) this just adds a bit of extra top whitespace, not a regression.

- **Timeline (Jul 2026, user idea — "a board game out there called Timeline... place cards into a sequence, guessing before/after... could we do the same with movies I've rated")** — `timeline.js` + `TimelineGame.vue`. A streak game (same shape as Higher or Lower: run ends on the first wrong guess, best streak persists to `settings/games/timelineBestStreak`), not a fixed puzzle: `start()` seeds a one-card timeline from a shuffled pool, then repeatedly shows a "mystery" card (poster, year hidden) that the player places into a gap in the growing timeline — either by tapping a gap directly or by dragging the card up into one (see the layout pass below).
  - **Pure module `timeline.js`**: `isValidPlacement(timeline, slotIndex, candidateEntry)` checks the candidate's year against its two would-be neighbors (inclusive comparisons — a tie year is valid on EITHER side of the matching boundary, since the player only ever sees a year, not an exact date, so an ambiguous tie shouldn't read as a wrong guess). `insertAtSlot` returns a new array (immutable, matching `sortResultsFast`'s decorate-sort convention). `correctSlotIndex` walks every slot and returns the first valid one — used only on a loss, to tell the player where the card actually belonged instead of just "wrong."
  - **Timeline invariant**: `timeline` is exclusively grown by inserting at the slot the player GUESSED, which by construction is only accepted when that slot is chronologically valid — so the array never needs re-sorting, and `isValidPlacement`/`correctSlotIndex` can assume ascending order rather than re-deriving it.
  - **Horizontally-scrolling row, not a growing-tall grid**: `timelineDisplayItems` interleaves gap/card/gap/card/…/gap (N cards → N+1 gaps) into one flat array rendered as a single-line, `overflow-x: auto` row — the exact same "constant height regardless of how long the sequence gets" convention Six Degrees' `.chain-row` established, reused here because Timeline has the identical "sequence only ever grows" shape.
  - **Banner, Claude-generated (Jul 2026 follow-up — "the banner images were created by Claude so maybe you could just make a new one that matches the style of the others")**: the other 4 banners were user-designed PNGs handed over from the downloads folder; no such source existed for Timeline. Rather than skip it, built one programmatically to match the established house style (deep-color background → halftone dot gradient through an accent color to cream → a smooth sine-wave "torn paper" edge → solid near-black bottom band → bold slab-serif game name + tracked-out "CINEMA ROLL GAMES" bottom-right) as an SVG generated by a small script, rendered to a `<canvas>` via an off-screen `Image()` load (sidesteps this environment's fixed mobile-emulation viewport, which made a direct DOM screenshot approach impossible - `resize_window` and `devicePixelRatio` didn't budge, so capturing at the real 1200×675 needed to happen in JS, not via the browser viewport), exported with `canvas.toDataURL()`, POSTed to a one-off local Python listener (`fetch` to `localhost:8935`) since the value was too large to reliably round-trip through a tool-call return, then decoded from base64 and run through the same `sips -s format jpeg -s formatOptions 82` pass as the user-provided ones. Color scheme (deep teal / amber / cream) and icon (a bold cream hourglass, amber "sand") were both chosen to be visually distinct from the other 4 (which cover red-orange, purple-pink, navy-gold, and yellow-red-black) while fitting the "time/history" concept. First attempt's torn edge used per-column pseudo-random jitter and looked spiky/noisy compared to the reference banners' smoother torn-paper look; replaced with a sum of 3 sine waves at different frequencies (`tornEdgeY(x)`) - critically, both the dot-cutoff logic AND the fill polygon sample the SAME function, so the two never visually misalign the way two independently-jittered edges did in the first pass. Wired in with the identical `created()`/`beforeUnmount()` `bannerUrl`/`hideHeaderLogo` swap the other 4 games use. Not committed to the repo: the scratch HTML/JS generator script (one-off, scratchpad-only) — only the final `timeline-banner.jpg` is.
  - **Layout tightened + drag-to-place added (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up bug report — rambled but decodable into five concrete asks)**: (1) the streak/best row was the very first thing on screen and read as too prominent — replaced with a small `.streak-line` ("Streak N · Best N") that now sits BELOW the status line instead of above/before it, so the status message ("Where does this belong?") is the top item. (2) Top padding trimmed `2.5rem → 1.75rem` (same tradeoff Six Degrees already made) — "move the whole thing up higher." (3) `.timeline-row` now renders ABOVE `.mystery-card` in the template (was below it) — "have the actual streak we're building be above the card... trying to add." (4) Already-placed `.timeline-card` tiles shrunk `84px → 62px` ("make the one that we know about a little bit smaller" — "the one we know about" = the revealed, already-placed cards, as opposed to the still-mystery one). (5) `.mystery-title`/`.mystery-year` removed entirely — the mystery card is now just the poster; correct/incorrect feedback on reveal is a border-color change on the card itself (green/red) instead of separate text, since the status line already states the title+year in prose.
    - **Reverted back to 84px (Jul 2026, later follow-up — "the timeline poster should be bigger... than they are right now")**: the mystery-card-25%-smaller change (below) never touched `.timeline-card` — the 62px size was this earlier, separate request — but got misremembered as connected. Restored to the original 84px (and `.timeline-gap` back to `126px × 34px` to match) regardless, since "bigger than right now" doesn't depend on which past request caused it.
  - **Drag-to-place — tried, then removed (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up bug report — "dragging is leaving trails everywhere")**: pointer-events-based dragging (drag the mystery poster up into a timeline gap) was built, but caused a visual trail/smear artifact on-device (`filter: drop-shadow` combined with per-`pointermove` transform updates repainting poorly — a known class of mobile rendering glitch, not investigated further once the user asked to just drop the feature). Removed outright: `onMysteryPointerDown/Move/Up`, `resolveDragSlot`, the `dragging`/`dragOffset`/`dragOverSlot`/`dragStartX`/`dragStartY` data fields, the `.timeline-gap.drag-over` highlight, and the now-unused `closestSlotIndex` pure export (deleted from `timeline.js` along with its tests — dead code, per this codebase's usual "remove promptly" convention). Tap-a-gap — always the parallel/fallback input path, never replaced — is now the only interaction. Same follow-up also shrank the mystery card another 25% (`190px → 143px`, on top of the smaller-than-original sizing from the round before).
  - **Timeline row centered, not left-stuck (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — "at the start of the game the timeline should be centered horizontally, not off to the left")**: `.timeline-row`'s `justify-content` changed from `flex-start` to `safe center` — plain `center` risks making the front of the row unreachable by scroll once it later grows wider than the screen in some engines; `safe center` centers while the content fits, then falls back to start-alignment (still fully scrollable, nothing stuck off-screen) once it doesn't.
  - **Gate**: `eligibleGameEntries.length < 5` (a placement game needs a real pool to draw from, though less than Connections' 16 or Six Degrees' well-connected-graph requirement) shows the same "help me get started" `NewRatingSearch` suggestions as the other three data-hungry games.
  - **Snappier reveal, two animation passes (Jul 2026, same follow-up round)**: the post-guess pause before the new card lands in the timeline and the next mystery card appears shortened `900ms → 400ms` ("there's a delay between clicking the spot and it showing up") — 900ms was copied from Higher or Lower's pacing, which has more to visually read (two full score reveals) than Timeline's single border-color flash.
    - *v1*: a CSS-only `animation: timeline-card-pop` on `.timeline-card` (scale+translateY+opacity), playing automatically since a newly-inserted card is a freshly-mounted DOM node with a brand new `v-for` key.
    - *v2, replaced it (immediate follow-up — "a sort of a motion to the slot... if it zoomed up there")*: v1's generic in-place pop didn't read as the card actually traveling anywhere. Replaced with a real FLIP (First-Last-Invert-Play) transition, `flyCardIntoSlot(cardKey, firstRect)`: `guess()` captures the mystery card's own `getBoundingClientRect()` (via a new `ref="mysteryCardEl"`) right as the 400ms pause ends — the "First" position/size — *before* it's replaced by the next mystery card. After Vue re-renders with the card in its new timeline slot, `flyCardIntoSlot` finds that exact DOM node (matched by `data-card-key` — a JS `dataset` comparison, not an interpolated CSS attribute selector, since `CSS.escape` isn't implemented in jsdom and a dbKey never needs escaping anyway), computes the delta between the two rects' centers plus a `scale` ratio from the width difference, and does the classic FLIP dance: set `transform`/`opacity` to the inverted "First" values with `transition: none`, force a synchronous reflow (`el.offsetHeight`) so the browser commits that state, then clear all three inline styles back to `''` in the same tick — the browser then animates from the committed inline values to the now-active CSS-declared natural ones. Reads as the poster shrinking and flying up from exactly where it was tapped into its slot. `.timeline-card`'s CSS `animation` was replaced with a plain `transition` (an `animation` wins the cascade over inline-style changes and would have fought the FLIP rather than played it). Falls back to no animation (card just appears) if either rect is zero-width — covers jsdom in tests and a genuinely hidden tab — so nothing crashes when real layout isn't available.
    - Test: `TimelineGame.test.js`'s FLIP case stubs `Element.prototype.getBoundingClientRect` to return two deliberately different, non-zero rects (mystery-card-sized vs. timeline-card-sized) to actually exercise the transform math, then asserts the placed card (found via its own `data-card-key`, since it can land before OR after the pre-existing seed card) ends with inline styles cleared back to empty — the same "settled" state a real browser would tween to. Every other test implicitly covers the zero-rect fallback path, since jsdom's real (unstubbed) geometry is zero everywhere.
    - *v3, replaced it (Jul 2026, bug report — "there's really no animation at all... I want it to look like the poster from the bottom slides up into the slot I chose and the slot gets larger to accommodate it")*: v2's FLIP was correct in its transform math (still passed its own test), but **never actually produced visible motion in a real browser** — the root cause: `el.offsetHeight` only forces a synchronous *layout* reflow, not a *paint*. For an element that's freshly `v-for`-inserted the same tick (no prior committed frame to transition away from), a single forced reflow isn't a strong enough guarantee that the browser paints the "from" state at all before the style is cleared — it can just collapse straight to the end state with zero visible tween. This is a well-known FLIP-on-new-elements gotcha; the fix is waiting for an actual committed paint, not just a layout pass.
      - **New design: a decoupled "flying" clone instead of FLIP-ing the real element**, which also directly delivers the "slot grows" half of the request (FLIP alone never touched the gap at all). `guess()` (now `async`) is a straight top-to-bottom sequence, not a `setTimeout` callback: capture `fromRect` (the mystery card's `getBoundingClientRect()`) and `toRect` (the TAPPED GAP's own rect, via `event.currentTarget` — `@click="guess(item.slotIndex, $event)"`) *before* anything moves → `await this.wait(150)` (a short pause so the green/red border registers) → if correct and both rects are real (non-zero, i.e. skip in jsdom/hidden-tab, same fallback spirit as v2): set `flyingCard` to an absolutely-`position:fixed` `<img>` sized/positioned to `fromRect` with `transition:none`, and `growingGapIndex = slotIndex` (drives `.timeline-gap.growing`, a plain CSS `width: 34px → 84px` transition — no JS math needed, the gap's own final width is a fixed constant) → **`await this.nextFrame()`** (two chained `requestAnimationFrame`s — NOT one, and not `el.offsetHeight` — this is the actual fix; two rAFs reliably guarantees a real committed paint of the "from" state before the flip) → reassign `flyingCard`'s style to `toRect`'s values WITH a real `transition` (`left/top/width/height`, 0.45s) → `await this.wait(450)` (the transition's own duration) → only THEN do the real data mutation (`insertAtSlot`, streak, best-streak dispatch, clear `flyingCard`/`growingGapIndex`, advance `mysteryCard`). The real `.mystery-card` is hidden (`v-if="mysteryCard && !flyingCard"`) the instant the clone takes over — since the clone starts pixel-identical to it, the swap is invisible. By the time the clone's flight finishes and clears, the target gap has already grown to the card's exact final width, so the newly-inserted real `.timeline-card` just fills in with no additional jump.
      - `.timeline-card` no longer carries any transform/opacity transition at all (that was v2's mechanism, driving the real element directly — no longer needed now that a separate clone does the animating). `wait(ms)`/`nextFrame()` are small reusable async helpers on the component now, not inlined into `guess()`.
      - Test: rewritten around the new sequence — stubs the same differing from/to rects, then asserts at each stage via `vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync`: right after the 150ms pause, `flyingCard` exists at `fromRect`'s values with `transition: 'none'`, `.mystery-card` is gone from the DOM, and `.timeline-gap.growing` exists; after the two-rAF window, `flyingCard` has flipped to `toRect`'s values with a `0.45s` transition; after the flight duration, `flyingCard`/`growingGapIndex` are both cleared and the card has actually landed in `timeline`. `vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync` was independently confirmed to fake `requestAnimationFrame` too (not just `setTimeout`), so this stays fully deterministic — no real wall-clock waiting or flakiness risk.
      - **Verification note**: automated screenshot-based live-browser verification of this specific change hit a real tooling limit — the automation tab reports `document.visibilityState === 'hidden'`, and Chrome throttles timers/rAF hard in backgrounded tabs, making sub-second motion impossible to sample reliably via that path (not a symptom a real, foregrounded user session would ever hit). Verified instead via the deterministic test above plus multiple live correct-guess playthroughs in the `testing-database` sandbox (each completed correctly end-to-end), including one screenshot that directly caught the gap mid-widen while the poster hadn't yet visually snapped into place.
    - *v4, rebuilt (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — "what actually happens is that the poster moves up as it get skinny... then it vanishes and pops up separately nearby. It's really not smooth")*: v3 blended position AND size into ONE simultaneous transition, landing the poster at the gap's exact rect. Two problems: (1) **a real, separate alignment bug** — `.timeline-row` used `align-items: flex-end`, but a `.timeline-card` (poster + year label below) is TALLER than a `.timeline-gap` (fixed height, no label), so bottom-aligning left a gap's TOP sitting visibly lower than a card's poster-image top. The clone landed at the (misaligned) gap's rect, then the REAL inserted card rendered at its own (correctly-aligned, but different) position — hence "vanishes and pops up nearby." Fixed: `align-items: flex-start` — gap and card poster tops now match exactly. (2) **The requested choreography is a different, explicitly 3-step SEQUENCE, not one blended motion**: "I want it to slide into the space of the plus sign slot, once it's there I want it to expand out until it's the right size. Then I want the two new plus sign slots to grow on either side of it. This should all happen in sequence." Also asked to run it deliberately slow (2s/step) purely to review and lock in the sequence before tuning speed.
      - **New `guess()` shape**: three named async helpers, awaited strictly in order — `flyToGap` → `expandInPlace` → (real data mutation) → `growInNewGaps`. All three durations come from ONE reactive `stepDurationMs` (currently `2000`, the "make it really slow for now" value — tune this single number once the sequence itself is confirmed correct), bound to a `--step-duration` CSS custom property on the component root so the JS `wait()`s and the CSS transitions can never drift out of sync with each other.
