        1. **`flyToGap`** — unchanged in spirit from v3's clone mechanism, but now travels to the tapped gap's OWN (narrow, 34px) rect rather than some blended final size — the poster genuinely "slides into the space of the plus sign slot," shrinking down to fit it, nothing more.
        2. **`expandInPlace`** — the clone is discarded; the poster now lives INSIDE the real, already-existing `.timeline-gap` DOM node at that exact position (`placingGapIndex`/`placingPosterUrl`, rendered via a `v-if` branch in the `v-for` that swaps that one gap's `<button>` for a `<div class="timeline-gap placing-gap">` hosting an `<img>`) — no jump, since the clone and this element occupy the identical spot. That REAL flex child then widens (`.wide` class, `width: 34px → 84px`) via a plain CSS transition — because it's a genuine layout element (not another position:fixed clone), neighboring gaps/cards reflow around it automatically via normal flexbox layout; no manual "where do the neighbors end up" math needed, unlike a fixed-position approach would require.
        3. **`growInNewGaps`** — after the real `insertAtSlot` mutation, the two gaps now flanking the new card (`[slotIndex, slotIndex + 1]` in the POST-insertion numbering — inserting at slotIndex always nets exactly these two indices, confirmed by hand-tracing a 2→3 card example) mount already collapsed (`.entering`: `width: 0; opacity: 0`), then grow in once that class is removed a frame later — same "needs a real committed paint before flipping, not just a reflow" reasoning as `nextFrame()` elsewhere in this file.
      - `isPlacing` (true for the ENTIRE 3-step sequence, not just step 1) replaced the old `!flyingCard` check gating `.mystery-card`'s visibility — with 3 discrete steps, `flyingCard` itself goes back to `null` well before the sequence finishes (steps 2/3 don't use it at all), so gating on just that would have let the real mystery-card flash back into existence at the bottom mid-sequence.
      - Test: the click-driven, `vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync`-based style still works cleanly for **step 1** (nothing precedes it, so there's no risk of a coarse advance call accidentally sweeping past a transient window). Steps 2 and 3 are triggered by the PRECEDING step's `wait()` resolving, which chains straight into scheduling fresh rAFs in the same continuation — a single coarse `advanceTimersByTimeAsync` call spanning that boundary was found (empirically, via a failing test) to sometimes sweep straight through the brief "just set up, not yet flipped" window in the same call that resolves the timer causing it. Splitting the advance into more, smaller incremental calls fixed step 2's test; step 3's still flaked the same way even split further, so `growInNewGaps` is instead tested by calling it DIRECTLY on the component instance (bypassing the full `guess()` chain entirely) — more precise anyway, and immune to this class of fake-timer cascade timing issue. A separate, simpler end-to-end test drives a full click through one large lump-sum advance and only asserts the final SETTLED state (timeline updated, streak, best-streak dispatch) — appropriate there since it doesn't care about any intermediate transient. Also added a suite-level `afterEach(() => vi.restoreAllMocks())` — a mid-test assertion failure previously left `Element.prototype.getBoundingClientRect` stubbed for whatever test ran next in the same file, causing a confusing, seemingly-unrelated failure in an untouched pre-existing test purely from mock leakage; discovered by running the failing test in isolation (`-t "..."`) and seeing it pass, which pointed straight at cross-test contamination rather than a real bug in that test.
      - **Verification**: local static build (not the user's own `yarn serve`) served on a separate port, tested against the `testing-database` sandbox. Directly observed live in a real browser: the "+" gaps now visibly top-align with poster images (alignment fix confirmed), and a full correct guess — poster visibly shrinks and slides into the narrow tapped slot, then (confirmed via the deterministic test, timing-difficult to catch via screenshot polling — see the v3 note above for why) expands to full size with the two new flanking gaps growing in, ending with a correctly-updated timeline and streak.
    - *v5, refined (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — "the plus sign elements aren't quite right. I think that adding the actual '+' is causing it to snap open and not look like it's growing")*: step 3's `.entering` class animated width AND opacity together, so the "+" glyph (a fixed-size character, clipped by the box's `overflow: hidden` while narrow) got revealed the instant the box was wide enough to stop clipping it — which happens almost immediately after `enteringGapIndices` clears (that's what STARTS the width transition, not something correlated with when it visually FINISHES), so the "+" popped in while the box was still visibly mid-grow rather than appearing on an already-settled box. Fixed with a SECOND, independently-timed flag: `plusHiddenGapIndices` is set alongside `enteringGapIndices` at the start of `growInNewGaps`, but — unlike `enteringGapIndices`, cleared after just one frame to kick off the width transition — stays populated for the gap's ENTIRE `stepDurationMs` grow duration, only clearing once the box has actually finished widening. The "+" text itself is conditionally rendered (`{{ plusHiddenGapIndices.includes(item.slotIndex) ? '' : '+' }}`) rather than opacity-faded, so there's nothing to clip/reveal mid-transition — it simply doesn't exist in the DOM until the box is already at its resting size. Border/background opacity-fade-in on `.entering` were left alone (not what was flagged — a growing bordered rectangle doesn't have the same "clipped glyph" problem a fixed-size character does).
      - Test: `growInNewGaps`'s direct-call test (already established in v4 to sidestep the fake-timer cascade issue) extended to assert `plusHiddenGapIndices` and the rendered gap text at all three points — immediately (both hidden, `''`), right after the collapsed-width class clears (still hidden, box now transitioning), and only after the full `stepDurationMs` elapses (revealed, `'+'`).
      - **Verification note**: live re-verification for this specific change hit the SAME `document.visibilityState === 'hidden'` tooling limit as v3/v4's note above, but more severely — this time `requestAnimationFrame` appeared fully SUSPENDED (not just throttled) for as long as the tab stayed backgrounded (confirmed stuck in an otherwise-correct mid-step-2 state — narrow poster already showing in the real gap, mystery card already hidden, `wide` class just not yet applied — even after 15+ real seconds), consistent with Chrome pausing rAF entirely for tabs that aren't actually being repainted, not merely deprioritizing it the way `setTimeout` is. Confirmed this is a real, understood browser behavior (not a code bug) rather than chasing it further — the state observed was exactly the correct "paused, waiting on a repaint that isn't coming" state, not an inconsistent one. Relied on the deterministic test above (unaffected, since vitest's faked `requestAnimationFrame` doesn't depend on real tab visibility) plus the same-session v4 live confirmation (a visible tab at the time) that the surrounding mechanism genuinely animates in a real browser.
    - *v6, two more real bugs fixed (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — "the poster disappears and the plus sign boxes appear on either side of a blank space. Then the poster reappears... they aren't expanding from 0 width they pop in with some amount of width and border and then grow")*: both traced to actual code defects, not just polish.
      1. **The blank-flash**: `expandInPlace` cleared its own state (`placingGapIndex`/`placingPosterUrl`/`placingWide` → null/false) as the LAST thing it did before implicitly returning. But `guess()` calls it via `await this.expandInPlace(...)` — and `await` always introduces at least one microtask tick before the calling function resumes, EVEN when the awaited async function's own work is already done. Vue's reactivity scheduler ALSO flushes via microtasks. Since `expandInPlace`'s state-clearing was what TRIGGERED a pending Vue re-render, and that trigger was queued strictly BEFORE `guess()`'s own continuation (which still needed the `await` to unwrap), Vue's flush reliably ran FIRST — painting a frame with the placing-gap gone (back to a plain "+") and the real card NOT YET inserted (since `insertAtSlot` hadn't run yet) — a real, visible intermediate frame, not a perception issue. **Fix**: `expandInPlace` no longer clears its own state at all; `guess()` now does that clearing in the SAME synchronous block as `insertAtSlot` and the step-3 `entering`/`plusHidden` setup — no `await` anywhere in between, so Vue batches all of it into one update/paint. General lesson for this codebase: an `await`-ed helper that both (a) mutates reactive state as its last act and (b) is immediately followed by MORE reactive mutations in the caller is a latent version of this exact bug — the two mutations need to be in the same synchronous block, not split across the helper-call boundary, whenever they're meant to paint together.
      2. **Gaps not growing from a clean 0**: `timelineDisplayItems` keyed gaps by raw array INDEX (`gap-${index}`). Index-based keys are unstable across insertion — inserting a card shifts every subsequent gap's index, so Vue's keyed diffing treated most gaps after the insertion point as "the same element, just moved" rather than tracking real identity. Concretely: of the two gaps newly flanking a placed card, AT LEAST ONE was actually a REUSED DOM node (one that had already been painted at a normal, non-collapsed width, e.g. from before the guess even started) rather than a fresh mount — applying `.entering` (width: 0) to an element with a real prior painted width triggers a genuine, visible shrink transition, and clearing it again a frame later means the "grow to 34px" starts from wherever that interrupted shrink left off, not from a clean 0 — exactly "pop in with some amount of width... then grow." **Fix**: gaps are now keyed by the identity of the card immediately preceding them (`gap-after-${entryKey(precedingCard) || 'start'}`), which is stable regardless of insertions elsewhere in the row. Inserting one card still means exactly one of the two flanking gaps coincidentally continues an old boundary's identity (inherent to splitting one gap into two — not a bug), but combined with fix #1 above (no more premature paint), that reused gap's last real committed state is from well before the guess even started (a normal, settled 34px "+"), so applying `.entering` to it now behaves the same as a fresh element functionally would.
      - Test: the direct-call unit test for the (renamed) `settleNewGaps` — split from the old `growInNewGaps` — now has the caller (mirroring what `guess()` actually does) set `enteringGapIndices`/`plusHiddenGapIndices` synchronously BEFORE calling it, since `settleNewGaps` itself only handles the async "wait a frame, clear collapsed, wait the grow duration, reveal +" half now — the synchronous "mark collapsed" half moved into `guess()` where it belongs (batched with the data mutation).
      - **Verification note**: this automation tab was `document.visibilityState === 'hidden'` from the very start of this round (same limitation as the v5 note), so live re-confirmation wasn't possible here either — and unlike v3-v5's issues (which were animation-timing/paint-order bugs `requestAnimationFrame` could eventually surface), this fix is purely about JS execution order within `guess()`, which live rAF-based visual polling wouldn't have isolated any more precisely than the code-level trace above already did. Left for on-device confirmation.
    - *v7 (Jul 2026, correction — the user clarified v6 didn't address what they actually meant): "the gap plus symbols... go from not existing to existing with a dotted border all at once. That initial jump from zero to a bordered element is jarring... I think that's partly because you're... expanding the width of the element, but the border is not included in that width."* Real, narrowly-scoped CSS bug: `.timeline-gap`'s `transition` property listed `transform`/`border-color`/`background`/`width`/`opacity` — but NOT `border-width`. `.entering` sets `border-width: 0` alongside `width: 0`, so when `.entering` is removed, `width` smoothly animates 0 → 34px over `--step-duration`, but `border-width` — not in the transition list — SNAPS instantly from 0 to its full 1.5px the very first frame, before the box has grown at all. First fix attempt: added `border-width` to the transition list so it'd animate alongside `width`.
    - *v8, replaced it (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — v7's fix didn't actually read as smooth: "It's still popping into existence with width. It needs to start from a single vertical line and grow gradually out to its width.")*: animating `width` + `border-width` directly together is still inherently jerky, and specifically worse for a DASHED border — the browser has to recompute the dash pattern's geometry on every frame as the box resizes, which doesn't interpolate as a clean, continuous visual the way a solid fill or a GPU-composited transform does. **Switched to `transform: scaleX()` instead of animating layout properties at all.** `.entering` now sets `transform: scaleX(0)` (collapses the WHOLE box — including its border, uniformly, since a transform scales the already-rendered box as one unit — to a vertical line at its center) and `opacity: 0`; removing the class transitions back to the implicit `transform: none` (scaleX(1)), growing smoothly outward from that center line in both directions. Layout `width` is now a CONSTANT `34px` at all times — never toggled — since only the VISUAL rendering scales, not the box's actual footprint; this also means there's no reflow to wait on for these two small gaps specifically (unlike step 2's `.placing-gap.wide`, which genuinely does need to reflow neighbors as the card-sized element grows — left untouched here, out of scope per "let's focus on that, and then we'll come back to the poster").
      - The base `.timeline-gap` transition rule's `transform` duration is now the slow `--step-duration` (needed for the entering→grown grow-out) — but `.timeline-gap:active:not(:disabled)` (the tap-press feedback, `scale(0.94)`) needs to stay snappy, so it now explicitly re-declares its own fast `transform 0.1s ease` transition, overriding the base rule's slower one for that specific interaction (the two never actually overlap in practice — entering gaps are `:disabled`, so `:active:not(:disabled)` can't match them anyway — but the explicit override keeps the intent unambiguous regardless).
    - *v9, reverted v8 and fixed the REAL bugs (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — "the space that the box occupies still pops open, and then the borders... grow into that space")*: this time diagnosed with hard evidence instead of another guess. The user recorded a phone screen capture and asked directly "how can I share a video... so you could see it, and you can verify for yourself" — `Read` cannot open video files (confirmed by trying), so frames were extracted via `ffmpeg` (installed via `brew install ffmpeg` — not present by default) and inspected as images, which surfaced the visual symptom but not a root cause. The DECISIVE step was live DOM inspection of the deployed build: `document.visibilityState` on the automation tab is `'hidden'`, which fully SUSPENDS `requestAnimationFrame` (not just throttles it, unlike `setTimeout`) — normally a testing obstacle, but exploited here on purpose: triggering a guess reliably FREEZES it mid-sequence forever (since `nextFrame()`'s double-rAF never resolves), giving a stable moment to inspect real `getComputedStyle()` output with no timing race. Later, `window.requestAnimationFrame` was monkey-patched to `setTimeout(cb, 16)` to let a full sequence actually complete for end-to-end checks.
      - **Bug 1 (the actual cause of "space pops open, then box grows into it")**: v8's `transform: scaleX()` approach kept `width` a CONSTANT `34px` specifically so the visible growth would be smooth — but transforms don't affect layout. That constant width means the full 34px of LAYOUT SPACE is reserved the instant the element mounts, before any animation runs at all; only the painted content inside that already-existing hole was ever animating. This is an inherent, unavoidable consequence of "constant width + transform," not a tuning problem — no amount of adjusting the transform can fix it, since the space and the content need to grow together as ONE thing. **Reverted to real `width`/`border-width`/`opacity` animation** (functionally back to v7's approach), so the reserved space and its visible content grow in lockstep.
      - **Bug 2 (found via direct `getComputedStyle` inspection, previously undiagnosed)**: `.timeline-gap.entering`'s `opacity: 0` and `.timeline-gap:disabled`'s `opacity: 0.35` have IDENTICAL CSS specificity (one class + one pseudo-class each) — and since entering gaps are ALSO always `:disabled` (`revealed` stays true for the whole placement sequence), both rules apply simultaneously, and `:disabled` won by later source order. A "collapsed" gap was silently rendering at 35% opacity, not 0, the entire time — confirmed directly: `getComputedStyle(gap).opacity` read `"0.35"` on a gap with `.entering` applied. **Fixed by selector, not reordering**: `.timeline-gap.entering:disabled` (three simple selectors instead of two) unconditionally outranks the plain `:disabled` rule regardless of source order, and is also more semantically honest — it names the real, always-true condition rather than leaving it as an implicit specificity tie.
      - **Bug 3 (found the same way, one edit later — this one's own follow-up bug)**: after fixing 1 and 2, `getComputedStyle` still showed `width: ~12px` for a "collapsed" gap, not `0`. Two compounding causes, both confirmed by testing each independently: (a) `.timeline-gap` is a flex item of `.timeline-row` with no `min-width` set — flex items default to `min-width: auto`, refusing to shrink below their own content's intrinsic size regardless of an explicit `width` declaration (added `min-width: 0`); (b) even with that fixed, `<button>` elements carry real UA-default padding that `content-box` sizing adds ON TOP of `width` — the leftover ~12px was exactly that default padding. Since the "+" glyph is centered via flex `align-items`/`justify-content`, not padding, `padding: 0` was added unconditionally to the base rule (safe for every state, not just entering) and the residual sliver disappeared — confirmed via the same `getComputedStyle` technique landing on a clean `{width:"0px", opacity:"0", borderWidth:"0px", padding:"0px"}`.
      - Also discovered along the way, unrelated to the animation itself but a real deploy-testing trap worth remembering: a plain `python3 -m http.server` + `navigate` on the SAME localhost port across successive rebuilds does NOT reliably serve fresh files — this app's own PWA service worker (registered from an EARLIER build at that exact origin) intercepts requests and serves its OLD precache regardless of what's actually on disk. Confirmed directly by reading `document.styleSheets` for the loaded `.entering` rule's `cssText` and finding it matched an EARLIER version of the code, with a stale `data-v-*` scope hash to match. Fix each time: `navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistrations()` → `unregister()` each, `caches.keys()` → `caches.delete()` each, THEN navigate again — verified fresh via the same stylesheet read showing a new `data-v-*` hash before trusting any further DOM inspection.
    - *v10, poster-swap flash fix (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — "there is often a flash between when the animated poster gets in place and when it gets replaced with the real poster... have the animated poster stay in place for longer until we're certain the real poster is fully loaded behind it")*: the sequence swaps between THREE separate `<img>` elements showing the placed movie's poster — the step-1 flying clone, step-2's `.placing-gap` poster, and the eventual REAL `.timeline-card` poster (a genuinely different, smaller URL — `w185` vs the `w342` the first two use) — and each swap creates a brand-new `<img>` node. Even with the SAME URL and a warm HTTP cache, a freshly-created `<img>` still has to be *decoded* before it can paint, which isn't always instant (especially on mobile) — that decode gap is what read as a flash.
      - **`preloadImage(url)`**: creates an off-DOM `Image()`, kicks off `HTMLImageElement.decode()` (falling back to `onload`/`onerror` if `decode` isn't available), and resolves once decoding is done — racing it against a 1.5s `wait()` cap. The cap isn't just test-friendliness: it's a genuine production safety net too, since a stuck or rejected decode should never be able to block the placement sequence forever (worst case the swap just isn't perfectly instant). Confirmed directly that this cap is NECESSARY, not just defensive — jsdom implements `decode()` but the returned promise never settles at all, hanging a first draft of this test indefinitely until the race was added.
      - **Both poster URLs are preloaded as early as possible** — right as the animated sequence begins, in parallel with (not after) the multi-second animation, so by the time either is actually needed the decode has almost always already finished and awaiting it costs nothing.
      - **The actual flash fix is in WHEN the outgoing visual gets cleared**, applying the exact same lesson from v6's blank-flash fix to this new case: `flyToGap` no longer clears `flyingCard` itself at the end of its own wait — that clearing moved into `expandInPlace`'s own synchronous prefix, bundled together with revealing the placing-gap poster (`this.flyingCard = null; ...; this.placingPosterUrl = posterUrl; ...`, no `await` between them). `guess()` awaits the large poster's preload BETWEEN `flyToGap` finishing and `expandInPlace` being called — since `flyToGap`'s own tail no longer touches any reactive state, that await introduces no premature Vue flush, so the flying clone visibly just sits still (exactly "stays in place for longer," per the request) for however long the decode-await takes, then disappears in the SAME instant the now-guaranteed-ready placing-gap poster appears.
      - Test: `preloadImage` gets its own direct unit test (resolves immediately for a null url; races correctly against the cap when the underlying decode/load never settles, confirmed via `vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync` before/after the 1.5s boundary). A stray `promise.then(() => {...})` in that test with no `.catch()`/return tripped `eslint-plugin-promise`'s `catch-or-return`/`always-return` rules — a real, correct lint catch (an unhandled rejection in a fire-and-forget `.then()` chain), fixed by adding `.catch(() => {})` and returning a value from the `.then()` callback.
    - *v11, timing pass begins (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — "everything is running smoothly, the animation looks great, so now we just need to adjust the timings... let's start by reducing everything down to half a second")*: `stepDurationMs` (2s → 500ms → 250ms, halved each round per direct follow-up feedback — "divide by two again") is confirmed correct in sequence and is now the tunable being dialed in for feel — a live, iterative experiment, not a one-shot value. The 1.5s `preloadCapMs` (see v10) is a genuinely separate, fixed constant — extracted from an inline literal to its own named `data` field specifically so it stays independent of however `stepDurationMs` gets tuned; it's a worst-case decode-safety-net, not part of the choreography's pacing, and is deliberately allowed to end up LONGER than a short `stepDurationMs` (at 250ms it's now 6x longer — the dynamic boundary-based test math below holds regardless, since it was never expressed as a fraction of `stepDurationMs`).
      - **Test-timing fallout, and the actual lesson**: once `preloadCapMs` (1.5s, fixed) exceeded the new `stepDurationMs` (500ms), it became the DOMINANT delay in the sequence — jsdom's `Image.decode()` never settles on its own (v10), so `await decodeLarge`/`await decodeSmall` in `guess()` always consume the full cap in tests, regardless of how fast the visual steps themselves are. The "step 2" test's checkpoint had been advancing the clock by a further `preloadCapMs` measured from the WRONG baseline (after step 1's own wait, rather than from when `preloadImage` was actually called) — with the old, larger `stepDurationMs` this slop was invisible (decodeLarge had always already resolved by then), but at 500ms it overshot straight through the intended "still narrow, not yet wide" checkpoint into a state several steps further along (a genuine test bug this refactor exposed, not a component bug). Fixed by computing the exact elapsed-time boundary at which `decodeLarge`'s cap timer actually fires (`400 + preloadCapMs` from the click — fixed, since that timer is anchored to when `preloadImage` is first called, independent of `stepDurationMs` entirely) and advancing precisely to it, with a comment spelling out why. The "full sequence end-to-end" test's lump-sum advance was widened the simpler way (`+ preloadCapMs * 2`, covering both preloads) since it only asserts the fully-settled end state and doesn't need the same precision.
    - *v12, settle+expand merged with the new-gap grow-in (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — "let's have the poster when it... after it slides up, I want it to expand and the plus sign gap markers to also expand at the same time")*: previously the poster's widen (old step 2, `expandInPlace`) fully finished BEFORE the real card was inserted, and only THEN did the two newly-created flanking gaps start their own separate collapse→grow (old step 3, `settleNewGaps`) — sequential, not simultaneous. Making them run together required inserting the real card earlier (so the flanking gaps' final positions/sizes exist to animate toward) — but the poster-widen visual relies on the tapped gap still being a GAP in `timelineDisplayItems` (`placingGapIndex` matches a `type: 'gap'` item), which is no longer true the instant the real card is inserted at that slot.
      - **Fix: extend the placing overlay to include the two new gaps too, purely visually, so the real insertion can stay a single, instantaneous, invisible swap at the very end** (unchanged architectural principle from v4-v10, just widened in scope). New `showPlacingFlanks`/`placingFlanksWide` render two `.timeline-gap.placing-flank` divs immediately before/after the `.placing-gap` poster — NOT tied to `timelineDisplayItems` at all, just extra flex children adjacent to it. `expandInPlace` was renamed `expandWithFlanks`: it now sets `showPlacingFlanks = true` alongside revealing the poster, and flips `placingWide`/`placingFlanksWide` true together after the SAME `nextFrame()`, so the poster and both flanks widen over one shared `wait(stepDurationMs)` — genuinely simultaneous, not two waits chained end to end. The now-fully-grown flanks are dropped (`showPlacingFlanks = false`) in the SAME synchronous block that inserts the real card — same "batch it or Vue paints an intermediate frame" discipline as every other handoff in this sequence.
      - **The old `.entering`/`plusHiddenGapIndices`/`settleNewGaps` machinery is gone entirely** (not just unused — removed): the real flanking gaps are never inserted in a collapsed state anymore, since by the time they're inserted the fake placeholders have already finished growing to the exact same resting size. `.timeline-gap.placing-flank` never renders a "+" at all; the real gap's "+" only exists once it's already fully-sized, which is the SAME timing relationship the old `plusHiddenGapIndices` delay used to enforce by hand (a real gap's `+` used to be hidden until its OWN grow finished) — it now falls out of the architecture for free instead of needing a dedicated hidden-then-revealed flag.
      - **Template gotcha**: rendering up to 3 conditional root nodes (flank-before, poster, flank-after) for a single `timelineDisplayItems` entry meant the existing single `:key="item.key"` on the outer `<template v-for>` could no longer safely stand in for the whole group — moved to explicit `:key` on every actually-rendered branch instead (the plain gap button and the card div each now carry their own `:key="item.key"`, the flanks get derived keys like `` `${item.key}-flank-before` ``), rather than relying on Vue's per-template key-forwarding across a variable number of sibling nodes.
      - Test: `TimelineGame.test.js`'s "step 2" test (renamed to reflect the merge) extended to assert `showPlacingFlanks`/`placingFlanksWide` and the two `.placing-flank` elements' collapsed state at the same "still narrow" checkpoint the poster was already asserted at, then their `wide` class alongside `placingWide` a frame later — proving simultaneity, not just that both eventually happen. The old standalone `settleNewGaps` unit test (which called a now-deleted method directly) was folded into that same test rather than kept separately, since there's no longer a separate step to isolate.
    - *v13, next mystery card fades in instead of popping (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — "the new poster... at the bottom for us to add in... make that fade in a little bit softer so it doesn't just pop into existence")*: the `.mystery-card` div's own `transition: border-color` (for the correct/incorrect flash) doesn't animate its ARRIVAL — a new mystery card is a freshly-mounted element (`v-if="mysteryCard && !isPlacing"` toggles false→true when a new card becomes eligible), and a plain CSS transition can't animate a property change that never happens on the same node, only a genuine mount. Wrapped the div in a Vue `<Transition name="mystery-fade">`; only an ENTER transition is defined (`.mystery-fade-enter-active { transition: opacity 0.4s ease; } .mystery-fade-enter-from { opacity: 0; }`) — leaving stays instant, unchanged, since only the arrival was reported as abrupt. Fixed 0.4s duration, deliberately NOT tied to `--step-duration`/`stepDurationMs` — this softens one specific pop, it's not part of the placement sequence's own choreography, so it shouldn't get faster as the steps above get tuned down further. `ref="mysteryCardEl"` (used by `guess()` to read the card's rect for the fly-to-gap animation) passes through the `<Transition>` wrapper fine, since Transition isn't a real DOM element.
      - No new test added specifically for the fade — jsdom doesn't run real CSS transitions, so a test could only assert the presence of the `mystery-fade-enter-*` class names for an instant, which wouldn't meaningfully guard against a future regression; the existing suite already exercises every path that mounts/remounts `.mystery-card` (initial start, after a correct guess, "Play Again"), so the wrapper's structural correctness (ref still resolves, classes still apply) stays covered by all 14 existing tests passing unchanged.
    - *v14, placed-card dates escalate to disambiguate ties (Jul 2026, bug report filed 2026-07-24 — "the timeline game should use actual release dates, not just years... if there are two movies from the same year they should show month and year, if there are two from the same month they should show day month and year")*: `formatTimelineDate(entry, timeline)` (new export in `timeline.js`) replaces the plain `movieYear(item.entry)` under each PLACED card only (`.timeline-year`) — checks the entry's year/month against every OTHER entry currently in `timeline` and escalates precision only as far as needed: unique year → `"1994"`; year shared with another placed entry → both entries escalate to `"Mar 1994"`; year AND month shared → both escalate further to `"Mar 10, 1994"`. Recomputed fresh on every render (not memoized), so precision correctly re-escalates if a LATER card creates a new collision with an already-placed one.
      - **Deliberately display-only** — `isValidPlacement`/`correctSlotIndex` are untouched and still compare by year alone ("the player only ever sees a YEAR... so two movies from the same year... should both read as correct," per that module's own pre-existing comment) — a tie still counts as valid either order even once month/day are now visibly shown for it. This is a real, acknowledged tension (a same-year pair placed "out of month order" will now visibly look non-chronological once month is displayed) but the bug report asked specifically about what's DISPLAYED, not the scoring rule, so the rule was left alone rather than unilaterally redesigned.
      - **Own date parsing, not `movieYear`**: `movieYear` (`gameUtils.js`) reads the year via `new Date(release_date).getFullYear()`, which parses as UTC midnight and can roll a day-1 date back into the PREVIOUS month/year in a negative-UTC-offset local timezone — a latent, previously-low-stakes quirk (only affects Jan 1, only shifts the YEAR) that would become directly visible and MORE exposed once month/day are shown for every day of the month, not just the 1st. `formatTimelineDate` instead regexes the `"YYYY-MM-DD"` string directly (`releaseDateParts`), sidestepping `Date` entirely — confirmed via a dedicated test with a real `1994-01-01` release date. `movieYear` itself was left unchanged (still used elsewhere in the component, e.g. the mystery card's reveal text) — fixing its underlying quirk was out of scope for a display-only request with a wider blast radius across other games.
      - CSS: `.timeline-year` gained `white-space: nowrap; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis;` as a safety net against the narrow 84px card — the longest string ("Jun 12, 1994", 12 chars) fits comfortably at the existing 0.7rem font size, but the same defensive truncation convention used elsewhere in Games (e.g. Reel Wordle's `.clue-value`) costs nothing to add.
      - Tests: `timeline.test.js`'s new `formatTimelineDate` suite (unique year, symmetric month escalation, symmetric day escalation, an unrelated third entry not bleeding into a tied pair, self-comparison excluded, the day-1 UTC-rollover case, missing/malformed date, missing `timeline` arg). `TimelineGame.test.js` gained one wiring test driving all three precision tiers through real `.timeline-year` DOM text.
    - **Housekeeping, same session**: checked `yarn fetch-bug-reports` for anything else outstanding. Two OTHER unresolved reports (both filed 2026-07-24, both about `TweakInline.vue`'s tiebreak posters — "no feedback when I tap a poster" and "posters change not at the same time... staggered") turned out to already be fixed by an EARLIER same-day commit (`5fee2da`, "Fix 4 tiebreak tournament bug reports: sticky hover, no prefetch...") — confirmed the checkmark-badge and `prefetchTournamentPosters` code from that fix is present in the current `TweakInline.vue` and already live in the deployed build (S3 `LastModified` postdates the fix commit). Marked both resolved via `yarn resolve-bug-report` rather than re-doing already-shipped work; most likely explanation is the reports were filed against a stale PWA-cached build the next morning, before the foreground update-check caught up. A third, genuinely still-open report (`HigherLowerGame.vue` still showed a redundant title label under each poster) was found and fixed immediately after, in v15 below.
    - *v15, redundant title label removed from Higher or Lower (Jul 2026, bug report — "we don't need to have labels underneath the posters the posters already have the title of the movie... we can keep the scores... but we don't need the labels")*: `.hl-card-title` (`{{ leftCard.movie.title }}` / `{{ rightCard.movie.title }}`, duplicating text already visible on the poster art itself) removed from both cards' templates and the now-unused CSS rule deleted. `.hl-card-score`'s own top margin (`0.4rem 0 0`) picks up the spacing the title used to provide between the poster and the score, so removing the label didn't leave the score sitting flush against the poster image. Test: `HigherLowerGame.test.js` gained a case asserting `.hl-card-title` no longer exists while `.hl-card-score` still renders (2 of them) — the score itself was never in scope for removal, only the label.
  - Tests: `src/test/games/timeline.test.js` (pure module — placement validity incl. ties/empty-timeline/no-derivable-year, immutable insertion, correct-slot lookup), `src/test/games/TimelineGame.test.js` (component — not-enough-movies gate, start state + no title/year text + timeline-before-mystery-card DOM order, correct guess grows streak + persists best + advances after the reveal delay + green border, wrong guess ends the run + highlights the true slot + red border, Play Again resets, a forced tie is accepted, running out of the whole library ends the game with a win-flavored message rather than a loss one, compact streak line replacing the old top row, banner set/restore + logo hide/show, the fly/grow animation sequence above). `GamesHub.test.js`'s tile-count assertion bumped 4 → 5.
- **Clue Budget (Jul 2026, user idea relayed from a friend)** — `clueBudget.js` + `ClueBudgetGame.vue`. Not a streak/puzzle game like the others: a fixed $100 budget spent on clues about ONE randomly-picked movie from the library, guessing it (via the same suggestions-dropdown pattern Reel Wordle uses) before the money runs out. "Reveal Poster" is always available and always costs whatever's left, full stop — an explicit give-up, per the friend's specific rule.
  - **What's actually available for a clue, and what isn't.** Before designing costs, checked what a stored `movie` object really carries (`AddRating.js`) rather than assuming full TMDB richness: title/year/runtime/genres/production_companies/keywords ARE persisted; `cast` is reduced to `{name, character}` (no gender/photo); `crew` is saved **unfiltered by job** (every TMDB credit, raw job strings like `"Director of Photography"`) — job-type extraction (Director/Writer/Composer/Cinematographer/Editor/Producer) only happens at read time via substring matching, which is why `gameUtils.js` gained `movieCrewByJob(entry, jobSubstring)` + five named wrappers (`movieWriters`/`movieComposers`/`movieCinematographers`/`movieEditors`/`movieProducers`, mirroring the existing `movieDirectors`, same loose-match convention `MovieDetail.vue`'s `getCrewMember` already established — e.g. `'Photo'` matches "Director of Photography"). **Tagline, overview/synopsis, TMDB vote average, budget/revenue, languages, and franchise/collection are NOT stored anywhere** (confirmed by a repo-wide grep, not an assumption) — the user specifically wanted tagline as a clue, so `ClueBudgetGame.vue` fetches it live, once per round (`GET /movie/{id}`, same `axios.get`+`api_key` pattern `NewRatingSearch.vue`/Six Degrees already use elsewhere), and folds it into the clue deck only if/when it arrives — best-effort, no tagline offered that round if the fetch fails or the movie genuinely has none on TMDB. Overview/synopsis was deliberately NOT added as a clue type even though it'd be easy to fetch alongside tagline — a plot summary is usually much more of an instant giveaway than marketing copy, out of step with "budget clues, don't just hand it over."
  - **Pricing evolved in three stages: hand-assigned tiers → billing-order fix → real dynamic pricing from live TMDB data.**
    1. *v1*: `buildClueDeck` assigned cost by how identifying a clue TYPE typically is — weak/broad info cheap (Decade $5, Genres $8), a direct fingerprint expensive (Director $20). Multi-value clues (cast, keywords) revealed one value at a time, cost escalating with reveal order.
    2. *v2 (bug report — "is cast member #1 first on the call sheet? Because they should cost more than someone further down")*: cast's original ascending pricing (`$15/20/25/30`, cheapest-first) had it backwards — `movie.cast` is saved in TMDB's own billing order, which roughly tracks real-world recognizability, so the TOP-billed actor is normally the single most identifying piece of cast info on its own. Flipped to `$30/25/20/15`. (Keywords stayed ascending — `flatKeywords` has no intrinsic per-item ranking the way billing order does, so reveal-order pricing is the only signal available for them at this stage.)
    3. *v3 (immediate follow-up — "what other tools do we have access to for dynamically pricing the other clues?")*: replaced hand-tuned/billing-proxy pricing with REAL signals wherever TMDB exposes one. See the dedicated section below.
  - **Multi-value clues (cast, keywords) still reveal ONE value at a time — `cast-0`/`cast-1`/`cast-2`/`cast-3` and `keyword-0`/`keyword-1`/`keyword-2` (capped at 3, lightly deduped by `computeFlatKeywords`'s own upstream logic) are each their OWN atomic deck entry, not one clue with an internal list** — modeling each reveal as its own purchasable item keeps the buy/purchased-list logic in the component uniform (every clue in the deck has exactly one `{key, label, cost, value}` shape, no special-casing for "clues with sub-parts").
  - **Dynamic pricing from real TMDB data (Jul 2026)**: three signals, one per clue category that actually has one available — asked and scoped explicitly via `AskUserQuestion` (options were people-only / people+keywords / people+keywords+company; user picked the full set).
    - **People** (cast, director, writer, composer, cinematographer, editor, producer) — TMDB's own `popularity` score, read directly off `GET /movie/{id}?...&append_to_response=credits`'s `credits.cast`/`credits.crew` (every person object in that response already carries `popularity` — no extra request beyond the one already needed for tagline). `priceFromPersonPopularity(popularity)` log-scales it (`5 + 4.5·log2(1+p)`, clamped `$8–$35`) since popularity has a long tail — a linear scale would make almost every real person look identically cheap. A multi-name group clue (co-writers, co-directors) prices off the MOST popular name in the group (`maxPopularity`) — if you'd recognize any one of them, the clue's done its job. This is what makes the v2 billing-order fix above obsolete as a proxy — cast is still OFFERED in billing order, but now priced by each person's own real popularity, correctly handling the case billing order gets wrong (a famous actor in a small/cameo role).
    - **Keywords** — TMDB has no popularity field for keywords, but `movie.keywords` (distinct from `flatKeywords`) stores the ORIGINAL TMDB objects with `{id, name}` (confirmed by reading `AddRating.js`, not assumed) — so for any offered keyword that came from TMDB (exact name match against `movie.keywords`), `GET /discover/movie?with_keywords={id}`'s `total_results` gives a real rarity signal: how many movies share it. `priceFromKeywordRarity(movieCount)` is the INVERSE curve from person pricing (`32 - 1.9·log2(n)`, clamped `$8–$30`) — rarer keyword, narrows the field more, costs more. AI-generated/custom keywords have no TMDB id at all and simply keep their fallback price (no lookup attempted).
    - **Production Company** — TMDB has no popularity field for companies either, and no better analog was available: `GET /discover/movie?with_companies={id}`'s `total_results` (via the id already sitting on `movie.production_companies[0]`) is a PROLIFICNESS proxy, not a fame score, so it's explicitly the noisiest of the three signals (a boutique studio like A24 isn't really "less famous" than Warner Bros., just less prolific) — priced with a narrower, lower band accordingly (`priceFromCompanyRarity`, `25 - 1.5·log2(n)`, clamped `$6–$25`) to reflect that lower confidence.
    - **No signal at all**: Decade, Exact Year, Runtime, Genres, Tagline stay fixed-price — none of these are TMDB "entities" with an id to look anything up by.
    - **Three independent fetches, fired in parallel, not sequenced** (`fetchLiveData` → `fetchTaglineAndPopularity` / `fetchKeywordRarity` / `fetchCompanyRarity`) so one slow endpoint doesn't hold back the others; each is best-effort (a failure just leaves that clue on its fallback price) and independently guards against a stale response landing after "New Round" already moved to a different target (`if (this.target !== roundTarget) return`, same pattern the tagline-only fetch already used).
    - **Fallback prices are the SAME numbers v1/v2 used** (`FALLBACK_COSTS`, `FALLBACK_CAST_COSTS`, `FALLBACK_KEYWORD_COSTS`) — a round is fully playable the instant it starts, before any network request resolves; `buildClueDeck(entry)` with no `extras` still produces a complete, sensible deck.
    - **Purchased clues are snapshotted at the moment of purchase, not re-derived from the live deck by key (`purchasedClues: []` of full clue objects, replacing the old `purchasedKeys: []` of just keys)** — this was a real bug the dynamic-pricing work would otherwise have introduced: `clueDeck` gets REBUILT every time a new live signal resolves, so a clue's cost can change after it's already been bought (e.g. Director bought on the $20 fallback, then the credits fetch resolves moments later and Director's live price becomes $27) — without snapshotting, the purchased-clues list would retroactively show the NEW price, not what was actually paid. `availableClues` (still-unpurchased) is unaffected by this and correctly keeps reflecting the latest live prices as they arrive.
  - **A guess is matched by identity (`entryKey`), not fuzzy title text** — unlike a free-text puzzle, a guess here is always tapped from the suggestions dropdown (real library entries, same UX as Reel Wordle's `submitGuess`), so it's always a real movie; comparing `entryKey(guess) === entryKey(target)` is simpler than string-normalizing titles AND correctly handles two different movies that happen to share a title (a remake, etc.) for free.
  - **The loss condition is unified**: buying clues that bring the budget to exactly $0, and tapping "Reveal Poster" (which forcibly sets budget to 0), both funnel into the same `lose()` — the poster button is just a manual, instant shortcut to a state that spending down to $0 organically would also reach. No separate "gave up" vs. "ran out of money" branching.
  - **Best score = money saved on a win** (`settings/games/clueBudgetBestSavings`, same persisted-best pattern as Higher or Lower's streak/Timeline's streak) — the more budget left when you guess correctly, the better; only overwritten when the new win is strictly cheaper-implied (higher leftover) than the existing best.
  - **Gate**: `eligibleGameEntries.length < 5`, same threshold and same "help me get started" `NewRatingSearch` fallback as Timeline.
  - **Banner**: same Claude-generated SVG→canvas pipeline as Timeline's (see above) — deep green background, gold halftone dots, a bold cream "$" coin icon (money-green fits "budget" thematically and was the only remaining unused color family across the 6 banners: red-orange/purple-pink/navy-gold/yellow-red-black/teal-amber/green-gold).
  - Tests: `src/test/games/gameUtils.test.js` gained coverage for the 5 new crew-by-job helpers (loose substring matching against an unfiltered, multi-job crew fixture). `src/test/games/clueBudget.test.js` (pure module — every field present → every clue present with the right cost/value, absent field → clue omitted entirely, cast/keyword caps, tagline only included when explicitly passed, every clue costs less than the starting budget, PLUS: `priceFromPersonPopularity`/`priceFromKeywordRarity`/`priceFromCompanyRarity` each monotonic in the right direction and clamped against extreme input, `buildClueDeck` actually uses dynamic pricing when `extras` provides it and falls back cleanly when a specific person/keyword is missing from the map, group clues price off the max popularity in the group). `src/test/games/ClueBudgetGame.test.js` (component — gate, round start incl. all three live fetches firing, popularity correctly differentiates cast-0 vs cast-1 cost, keyword rarity only fetched for a TMDB-id-bearing keyword, company rarity fetch, a clue bought before live data resolves keeps its fallback price even after later live data arrives — the purchased-snapshot fix, buying deducts budget + moves to purchased/blocks double-buy/blocks unaffordable, spending to exactly $0 auto-loses + reveals poster, "Reveal Poster" always drains everything, correct guess wins + persists a new best, wrong guess doesn't end the round, a worse win never overwrites a better existing best, New Round resets everything and re-fetches fresh live data, suggestion filtering, banner set/restore + logo hide/show). `GamesHub.test.js`'s tile-count assertion bumped 5 → 6.
- **Tagline Guess (Jul 2026, user idea — "I really like seeing the tagline for a movie and then trying to guess the movie... any ideas of how to make that into a fun game?")** — `taglineQuiz.js` + `TaglineQuizGame.vue`. First offered a more elaborate Wordle-style typed-guess design; the user redirected to something simpler: "I wanna see a tagline, and then I wanna see four movie posters, and I have to choose which of the four movies had that tagline." A streak game, same shape as Higher or Lower (correct → streak grows, continue; wrong → run ends, "Play Again"); best streak persists to `settings/games/taglineQuizBestStreak`.
  - **Tagline isn't stored locally** (confirmed elsewhere in this file, under Clue Budget's own tagline clue) — fetched live per round via the SAME `GET /movie/{id}?api_key=...&language=en-US` call Clue Budget already uses. Since there's no way to know AHEAD of a fetch which of the user's movies actually has a non-empty tagline, `startNewRound()` picks a random eligible movie, fetches, and on an empty/missing tagline retries with a DIFFERENT random movie (excluded via a `Set` of already-tried keys), up to 8 attempts before giving up and showing a "couldn't find a movie with a tagline — try again" retry state (`noTaglineFound`) rather than hanging indefinitely.
  - **Stale-fetch guard**: a `roundId` counter is bumped at the start of every `startNewRound()` call; the sequential retry loop checks the locally-captured `myRoundId` against `this.roundId` after each `await` and bails silently if a NEWER round has since started (e.g. the player double-tapped "Try Again" before the first attempt's fetch resolved) — same idiom Clue Budget's own fetch methods already use (`if (this.target !== roundTarget) return;`), just applied per-loop-iteration here since this game retries sequentially rather than firing one fetch per clue in parallel.
  - **Options**: `buildRoundOptions(targetEntry, pool, rng)` (pure, `taglineQuiz.js`) — the target plus 3 random distinct decoys via the existing `pickRandomDistinct`, all shuffled together (not target-first) so its on-screen position varies round to round. A 2×2 poster grid, no title labels underneath (same "the poster already shows the title" reasoning as Higher or Lower's own label removal earlier this session).
  - **Feedback reuses Higher or Lower's checkmark/X badge convention directly** (`.tq-guess-badge`, green check / red X, corner-positioned on the tapped poster) rather than a color change — on a WRONG guess, the badge ALSO appears on the actual correct poster (green check) so the player sees where the real answer was, same "reveal the true answer" pattern Timeline's `correctSlotOnLoss` already established.
  - **Real bug caught by its own test, before shipping**: `startNewRound()` (the "fetch the next round" primitive, used BOTH to advance after a correct guess AND — mistakenly — by the "Play Again"/"Try Again" buttons) never reset `streak`. Advancing after a win correctly needs to PRESERVE the streak, but restarting via "Play Again" after a loss needs to ZERO it — conflating the two meant losing a run and hitting "Play Again" silently kept whatever streak you'd had before the loss. Fixed by splitting out a `playAgain()` method (`streak = 0` then `startNewRound()`) as the ONLY thing the reset buttons call; `startNewRound()` itself never touches `streak`. Caught by a test that deliberately wins a round first (to get a real non-zero streak), loses the next one, and asserts the streak is still non-zero right up until "Play Again" is tapped — a test that only checked the STARTING state (0) wouldn't have caught this, since 0 stays 0 either way.
  - **Renamed to "Tag" + got a custom banner (Jul 2026, follow-up)** — the concept stuck, so it got the same treatment the other 6 games have: `created()`/`beforeUnmount()` now swap `bannerUrl`/`hideHeaderLogo` exactly like the rest (previously skipped entirely, "first build, not yet validated as a keeper"). `src/assets/images/games/tag-banner.jpg` — same house style as the others (halftone-dot gradient, torn-paper edge, bold slab-serif title, tracked "CINEMA ROLL GAMES" bottom-right) but generated directly as an SVG (Python script, not committed — one-off scratch) and rasterized with `rsvg-convert`, rather than the canvas/screenshot pipeline Timeline's banner used — simpler and didn't need a live browser session. Deep plum/crimson halftone, distinct from all 6 other banners' color schemes. Icon went through one revision: a quotation mark (tying it to the actual mechanic — guessing a movie from a quoted tagline) was tried first but didn't land ("I don't love the quotation mark"); replaced with a literal tag/label icon (Bootstrap Icons' `bi-tag-fill` glyph, traced/scaled directly into the SVG) — a more direct, unambiguous match for the new one-word name. `GamesHub.vue`'s tile `name` changed from `'Tagline Guess'` to `'Tag'` — the internal route (`/games/tagline`), component name (`TaglineQuizGame`), and file name are all UNCHANGED (only the user-facing display string), so `LAST_PLAYED_KEY`/bookmarks/etc. are unaffected.
  - **Posters laid out in a single row of 4, not a 2×2 grid (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — "all four posters show up on a single screen... nothing, I don't have to scroll to see anything... they could be side by side across the screen instead of being larger and two by two")**: `.tq-options-grid` changed from a 2-column grid (`1fr 1fr`, `max-width: 420px`) to `grid-template-columns: repeat(4, 1fr)` at `max-width: 380px`, sized down to roughly Timeline/Six-Degrees-tile territory (~85-90px wide posters on a typical phone). The actual fix for "fits without scrolling" is mostly a side effect of going from 2 ROWS of posters to 1 — a single row is naturally about half the total vertical height a 2×2 grid was, which is what actually frees up room for the rest of the page (tagline, status line, streak line) to fit on screen too. Paired with modest, secondary trims elsewhere on the page (root `padding-top` 2.5rem → 1.75rem matching the standard trim other games already got; `.tagline-text`'s font-size/margins/`min-height` all reduced slightly) as extra headroom, not the primary fix.
  - **Decoys chosen for thematic similarity, not pure randomness (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up — "try to find movies that sort of could have a similar tagline... something with keywords... a little bit more difficult... several plausible options")**: `buildRoundOptions` now scores every OTHER library movie against the target by shared keywords/genres (`similarityScore`, keyword overlap weighted 3x a genre overlap — a specific shared keyword like "heist" is a much stronger "these two could plausibly be confused" signal than a broad shared genre like "Crime", which dozens of movies share) and picks the 3 HIGHEST-scoring movies as decoys, not 3 random ones. Comparing actual TAGLINE text (the user's own first suggestion) was deliberately not pursued — every other candidate's tagline would need its own live TMDB fetch just to compare, multiplying this game's network calls per round for a signal that's also just harder to compute anything meaningful from (short marketing copy, no shared vocabulary structure) than keywords, which are already local, structured, and reused as-is from a pure module (`computeFlatKeywords`, `src/utils/keywords.js` — the exact same one Connections' keyword categories already use) rather than reinvented.
    - **Ties broken by a pre-sort shuffle, not library order**: `others` is shuffled BEFORE being scored+sorted (`Array.prototype.sort` is stable, so that shuffle — not the sort — is what actually supplies randomness among equal scores). Matters most for the common case where a target shares no keyword/genre with anything: without it, the same handful of library-order-first entries would win the "tie" every single time that target came up, defeating the whole "several plausible options... round to round variety" goal for exactly the movies where similarity has nothing to offer anyway.
    - Tests: `taglineQuiz.test.js` — the 3 highest-scoring movies win over 10 unrelated ones across 20 draws (proves it's not probabilistic/sometimes-random, it's deterministic top-3 selection with only tie order varying), a shared keyword outweighs a shared genre when only one decoy slot is left to fill, and the pre-existing structural tests (distinct decoys, no target-as-decoy, shuffled display position, no pool mutation) all still pass unchanged against keyword/genre-aware fixtures.
  - **Gate**: `eligibleGameEntries.length < 4` — the lowest bar of any game here (just enough for one target + 3 decoys), matching the user's own "won't be very difficult" framing for a casual multiple-choice format.
  - Tests: `src/test/games/taglineQuiz.test.js` (pure module — target + 3 distinct decoys, target never duplicated as a decoy, display order genuinely varies across draws, no input mutation). `src/test/games/TaglineQuizGame.test.js` (component — gate, round auto-starts on mount with a real tagline + 4 options including the target, correct guess badges+grows streak+persists best+auto-advances after 900ms, wrong guess badges both the tap AND the real answer + ends the run, "Play Again" resets the streak bug above, the no-tagline-found retry state, the stale-fetch guard). `GamesHub.test.js`'s tile-count assertion bumped 6 → 7.

- **Trivia (Jul 2026, user idea — "a straight trivia game... it picks one movie and then goes and looks up interesting trivia about the movie. Then asks a series of decreasingly difficult questions until the player guesses it... ideally, we would not be just using TMDB data")** — `trivia.js` + `TriviaGame.vue`. The user explicitly wanted trivia richer than structured TMDB fields (the exact thing Clue Budget's clues deliberately stick to) — this app already has a small Claude-backed AI lambda (`aws-lambda/claude-ai.js`, `VUE_APP_AI_API_URL`) used for RateMovie's "context" modal and ChatGPT-named-but-actually-Claude keyword suggestions, so a third route was the natural fit rather than inventing new AI infra.
  - **New `/trivia` lambda route** (`getTrivia`): asks Claude for exactly 5 true, self-contained trivia facts about the movie, explicitly ordered HARDEST/most-obscure first → EASIEST/most-identifying last, instructed never to name the movie's title and to prefer behind-the-scenes/production/casting/reception trivia over dry facts (runtime/budget/date) the game already has other access to. Returns `{ facts: [...] }`. **Requires a manual lambda redeploy** (re-zip `aws-lambda/` + upload) — the AWS credentials available in this dev environment don't resolve to the account/region hosting the real function (`list-functions`/`get-apis` came back empty against the URL in `.env`), so this couldn't be deployed automatically; the client code degrades gracefully (see below) until it is.
  - **Flow, unlimited guesses, no hard loss state** (matching this session's established "get rid of the limited number of guesses" precedent from Connections/Reel Wordle): one fact is shown at a time, starting with the hardest. A guess is always picked from the player's own library via the same typeahead-suggestions pattern Clue Budget/Six Degrees use (never free text) — a WRONG guess reveals the next fact, exactly like tapping "Next Clue" does; both just advance the same `revealedCount`, so the score doesn't care which path got you there. "Reveal Answer" is always available as an explicit give-up (status `'revealed'`, no score). Score is golf-style — fewer facts needed to guess correctly is better — persisted as `settings/games/triviaBestFactsUsed` (lower replaces higher, `isNewBestScore`).
  - **`pickTriviaTarget(pool, excludeKey, rng)`** — the same "exclude whatever the previous round's target was, fall back to the full pool if that would leave nothing" shape Clue Budget/Tag already use inline, pulled out here as its own directly-tested pure function specifically because TWO other games' "keeps repeating" bug reports (see the section above) both traced to this exact exclusion being missing or incomplete elsewhere — built in and tested from day one this time, not bolted on after a report.
  - **Real bug caught by the component's own tests, before shipping**: the stale-fetch guard (`if (this.target !== roundTarget) return;`) compared the reactive-proxy-wrapped `this.target` against the RAW object reference captured in `roundTarget` before it was ever assigned to `data()` — Vue lazily wraps a plain object in a new Proxy the first time a reactive property holding it is read, so this comparison is never `true` once that wrapping has happened, permanently stalling `loading` and leaving `facts` empty forever. Confirmed directly (`reactive({target:null}).target = raw; state.target === raw` → `false` in a real Vue instance). Fixed by comparing `entryKey(this.target) !== entryKey(roundTarget)` (a primitive string read, unaffected by proxy wrapping) instead of object identity.
    - **The identical pattern already existed in `ClueBudgetGame.vue`'s three live-TMDB-fetch methods** (`fetchTaglineAndPopularity`/`fetchKeywordRarity`/`fetchCompanyRarity`, all `if (this.target !== roundTarget) return;`) — found and fixed the same way while investigating this. Its own test file had ZERO assertions on the live-merge SUCCESS path (only on fallback pricing, which looks identical whether the guard is legitimately correct or permanently stuck true) — added one (`ClueBudgetGame.test.js`, "live TMDB data actually merges into the clue deck once the fetches resolve") that would have caught it. **Real-world severity is genuinely uncertain, not confirmed broken in production**: Vuex 4 wraps its own state in `reactive()` internally, so in the real app `roundTarget` is very likely ALREADY a Vuex-reactive proxy by the time it's captured (assigning an already-reactive value to another reactive property is idempotent in Vue 3, preserving identity) — this bug reliably reproduces against this test suite's plain (non-Vuex) mock stores, which is how it was actually found, but may or may not have ever manifested for a real logged-in user. Fixed regardless since the `entryKey`-based comparison is strictly more correct and is now actually testable.
  - **Gate**: `eligibleGameEntries.length < 5`, same threshold as Clue Budget.
  - **Banner**: same Python/SVG → `rsvg-convert` → `sips` pipeline as Tag's (no live browser session needed). Royal-blue → navy halftone gradient with a bold cream "?" icon and gold accents — a "quiz show" palette distinct from all 7 other banners' color schemes.
  - **Progress persistence, added same day (bug report — "I started the trivia game and then went to the home screen and then came back it reset")**: Trivia originally had NO persistence at all, unlike every other game with a resumable round (Reel Wordle/Six Degrees/Connections/Timeline) — leaving and returning always looked like a "reset" because it genuinely was one, a brand-new random target every single mount. Follows the exact `localStorage`-key pattern those games already use (`cinemaRoll.trivia.current`), with one real difference: **the facts THEMSELVES are persisted, not re-derived** — unlike Reel Wordle's clues (a pure function of the target, safely recomputable), Trivia's facts come from Claude and aren't deterministic, so re-fetching on restore could hand back a different set than what the player already saw. `persistState()` is one shared gate every mutating method funnels through (`revealNextFact`/`win`/`giveUp`/`startNewRound`/`fetchFacts`'s success branch): it only WRITES when `status === 'playing'` and there are real facts to save; any other call (an already-won or already-revealed round, or a round with nothing fetched yet) clears the key instead — so a finished round is never resumed (matching the "don't resume a finished round" rule established by Six Degrees/Reel Wordle/Connections) without needing a separate "was this won" check on restore. `loadOrStart()` (replacing the watcher's direct `startNewRound()` call) looks up the saved entry, re-resolves the target by key against the CURRENT `eligibleGameEntries` (falling back to a fresh round if it's gone — unrated/removed since), and restores `facts`/`revealedCount` directly from storage with no network call.
    - **Investigation before the fix**: extensive live testing (multiple navigation paths — the Home-button shortcut, a full BackLink hub→Home→hub round trip, leaving mid-fetch) could not reproduce a permanently *stuck* load, only the confirmed-and-now-fixed "always resets" behavior. If a stuck/blank state resurfaces after this fix, it's a separate issue — worth checking `navigator.serviceWorker`/cache state on the affected device first, since a botched deploy earlier the same day (see the "Two version-corruption incidents" note elsewhere, if present, or ask the user to try a hard refresh / force-quit-and-reopen) could plausibly explain a transient bad client-side cache unrelated to this component's own logic.
    - Tests: a new persistence describe block in `TriviaGame.test.js` — restores an in-progress round (facts + revealed count) across a remount with NO re-fetch, does not resume a won round, does not resume a revealed/given-up round, "New Round" overwrites stale saved progress rather than leaving the abandoned round resumable, and falls back to a fresh round when the saved target movie is no longer eligible. `beforeEach` gained `window.localStorage.clear()` — same cross-test-leakage guard Connections/Reel Wordle/Six Degrees' own test files already needed, since fixtures reuse the same dbKeys across tests.
  - Tests: `src/test/games/trivia.test.js` (pure module — target exclusion/fallback, revealed-count clamping, best-score comparison). `src/test/games/TriviaGame.test.js` (component — gate, round auto-start showing just the first fact, wrong guess reveals the next fact, "Next Clue" reveals without guessing and disables once exhausted, correct guess wins + shows poster/title + persists a new best, a worse score never overwrites a better existing best, "Reveal Answer" ends the round without a score, "New Round" excludes the just-finished target, the fetch-failure retry state + "Try Again"/"New Movie", the stale-fetch guard, banner set/restore + logo hide/show, progress persistence). `GamesHub.test.js`'s tile-count assertion bumped 7 → 8.

Router: `/games` (hub) + `/games/higher-lower` / `/games/wordle` / `/games/connections` / `/games/six-degrees` / `/games/timeline` / `/games/clue-budget` / `/games/tagline` / `/games/trivia`, all `requiresLogin`. Entry point: a controller-icon button in Home's results-actions row (`goToGames()`) — see the Grouped Result Hierarchy section above for why it moved into the slot the group-order button used to occupy.

- **Resume-last-game + preserved search (Jul 2026, bug report — "make the games button take me back to whatever game I was playing")**: `goToGames()` used to always push `/games` (the hub), so popping over to check a game and back meant re-picking it every time. `gameData.js` (the shared mixin every game component uses) now exports `LAST_PLAYED_KEY` and, in a `created()` hook, records `this.$route.path` to `localStorage` — guarded to skip `/games` itself (GamesHub also mixes this in for its eligibility gate, but visiting the hub isn't "the game you were playing"). `goToGames()` reads that key and pushes it instead of the hub, falling back to `/games` if nothing's stored or the stored value isn't an actual game route (`startsWith('/games/')`). Separately, `Home.vue`'s `beforeRouteLeave` used to only persist search state (`homePageSearchValue`/`homePageSearchChips`/etc.) when navigating to `MovieDetail`; it now also does so for any `/games` destination, so a direct "back to home" link from within a game resumes the exact search you had (the *restore* side, in `mounted()`, was already origin-agnostic — only the save side needed widening). `ReelWordleGame.vue` got a `.home-shortcut` button (top-right corner, mirroring `BackLink`'s top-left corner-lift trick) as that direct link, alongside the existing `BackLink` ("Games", top-left) which still goes to the hub. Tests: `src/test/GamesNavigation.test.js` (mounts `Home.vue`, following `BannerResolution.test.js`'s mock-store pattern).
  - **Hub visit clears the "last played" record (Jul 2026, immediate follow-up bug report)**: `LAST_PLAYED_KEY` only ever got overwritten by visiting ANOTHER game, never cleared — so a player who finished a round and deliberately tapped `BackLink` back to the hub (signaling "let me choose again") still got dropped straight back into that same completed game from Home's shortcut. `GamesHub.vue` now has its own `created()` that removes `LAST_PLAYED_KEY` — runs AFTER `gameDataMixin`'s own `created()` (Vue merge order: mixin hooks fire before the component's own of the same name), which itself already skips writing anything for the hub's own `/games` route, so there's no race between the two.

**`BackLink.vue` lifts itself up over the global Header via CSS, not by hiding it (Jul 2026 fix)**: every game screen + the hub use `<BackLink>` as their sole top-of-page affordance (same "◀ Home"/"◀ Games" caret pattern as MovieDetail/RateMovie/Insights' `.home-link`). Initially fixed by hiding the global Header (`setShowHeader(false)`, the MovieDetail/RateMovie approach), but the user wanted the Header to stay visible instead — same as **Insights.vue**, which never touches `setShowHeader` at all. Insights achieves "the link lives up in the header" purely via CSS: `.home-link` is `position: absolute; top: 6px; left: 6px` with **no positioned ancestor anywhere up its tree** (`.insights` itself has no `position` set), so it escapes to the page's initial containing block instead of the component's own content flow, landing it up over the Header rather than below it. `.back-link` in `BackLink.vue` now uses the identical trick (confirmed none of the 7 game-component wrapper divs set `position` either, so nothing accidentally re-scopes it locally). Test: `src/test/games/BackLink.test.js`.
- **`BackLink` gained a translucent dark background (Jul 2026, bug report — "hard to see... give it the same dark translucent background that we have on the cinema roll title").** Plain `color: #eee` text with no backing was sometimes illegible depending on the banner image behind it. `.back-link` now has `background: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.6); border-radius: 6px; padding: 4px 10px;` — same `rgba(0,0,0,0.6)` value `Header.vue`'s `.home-link` (the "Cinema Roll" title) already used, applied as a small pill rather than matching `.home-link`'s flush-corner single-radius shape 1:1, since `.back-link` sits with a 6px offset rather than flush against the true corner.
- **`ReelWordleGame.vue`'s top-right `.home-shortcut` button removed entirely (Jul 2026, bug report — "I don't need that... we don't have that anywhere else... the way you get to Home is by clicking the title").** This was a Wordle-only direct-to-`/` shortcut added alongside `BackLink` ("Games", top-left); per feedback it was inconsistent with the rest of the app, which relies on tapping the "Cinema Roll" header title to go home. Removed the button and its scoped CSS; the `beforeRouteLeave` search-state-preservation widening for `/games` destinations (see "Resume-last-game + preserved search" above) stays — it's independently useful for the `BackLink` → hub → Home path too, not specific to the removed shortcut.
- **`BackLink` gained a left-edge swipe-to-go-back gesture (Jul 2026, bug report — "I should be able to swipe left from the left edge of the screen in order to trigger the same back behavior").** Since all 5 screens (4 games + the hub) route through this one shared component, the gesture was added once here rather than per-screen. `BackLink.vue` is now a two-root template (Vue 3 fragment): a new `.back-link-edge-swipe` div (`position: fixed`, full viewport height, 20px wide, pinned to the left edge, `z-index: 500`) alongside the existing `.back-link` pill. It tracks `touchstart`/`touchend` (deliberately `@touchmove.passive="() => {}"` — bound but a no-op, and passive/no `preventDefault` so normal vertical scrolling under that 20px strip is never blocked) and emits the same `'click'` event a tap on the link itself would when the gesture reads as a real rightward swipe (`deltaX > 60`, `Math.abs(deltaY) < 50` — mostly horizontal, far enough to not be a stray tap on the thin strip). Starting the touch anywhere in the 20px-wide zone is what stands in for "started at the edge" (no separate distance-from-edge check needed). **Multi-root gotcha**: adding the second root element broke `wrapper.trigger('click')` in the existing test — with a fragment, Vue Test Utils' `wrapper.element` resolves to the FIRST root node (now the swipe zone, which has no click handler), not `.back-link` — fixed by triggering on `wrapper.find('.back-link')` explicitly. Test: `src/test/games/BackLink.test.js` (swipe emits click; a short tap-length touch, a mostly-vertical drag, and a leftward swipe all correctly do NOT).
- **Wordle guess suggestions now show the release year (Jul 2026, bug report — "I don't really know what year that movie came out").** `.suggestion-item` renders `{{ entry.movie.title }} ({{ suggestionYear(entry) }})`; `suggestionYear` reuses the same simple `new Date(release_date).getFullYear()` extraction `MovieDetail.vue`'s `getYear` already uses elsewhere, for consistency (same known timezone-edge-case caveat as that method — not a new issue introduced here).
- **...and separately, so does the guess-history card itself (Jul 2026, same-day correction — "I meant... the card that shows up after I guess a movie", not the typeahead dropdown above).** `.clue-title` (the guessed movie's name atop each `.clue-row`) now always shows `clue.year.value` in a muted `(YYYY)` span — that field already held the guess's own actual release year (via `compareNumber`'s `{ value: guessValue, ... }`, unconditionally, regardless of match), the template just wasn't rendering it there. Deliberately independent from the existing "Year" **clue cell** in the same row, which is the real game mechanic (an arrow when wrong, the number only revealed on an exact match against the target) — that comparison behavior is untouched; this is purely an always-visible label for what you just typed, not another hint about the target.
- **GamesHub now scrolls to top before navigating into a chosen game (Jul 2026, bug report — picking a game while scrolled down in the tile grid left the new game's page scrolled down too).** Vue Router has no global `scrollBehavior` configured (and adding one risks conflicting with `Home.vue`'s own intentional scroll-position preservation for movie-detail navigation), so this is a narrow, local fix: `selectGame(path)` calls `window.scrollTo({ top: 0, behavior: 'instant' })` before `this.$router.push(path)`, replacing the tile buttons' inline `$router.push`.
- **Connections keyword categories now have a MINIMUM shared-movie count, not just a maximum (Jul 2026, bug report — "needs to be more than a certain number... but also less than a certain number, sweet spot").** New `KEYWORD_MIN_MOVIE_COUNT = 5` alongside the existing `KEYWORD_MAX_MOVIE_COUNT = 10`. Reasoning is more than just "feels more obscure": a keyword shared by exactly `GROUP_SIZE` (4) movies has zero flexibility during puzzle generation — all 4 must be claimed as-is, with no ability to swap one out if it overlaps a different category's pick — making that category both a coincidental-feeling group AND more likely to get skipped entirely by the greedy no-overlap claiming step. `buildCandidateCategories`'s keyword filter is now `>= KEYWORD_MIN_MOVIE_COUNT && <= KEYWORD_MAX_MOVIE_COUNT`.
- **Connections gained title-text categories (Jul 2026, user idea — "get a little weird with it... number of syllables... starts with a letter... single-word titles").** New `title` kind, tier 3/blue (alongside director/cast — a title pattern is immediately verifiable once you suspect it, unlike keyword's need to recall a movie's theme; a judgment call, not a precise measurement). Two sub-categories, both in `connectionsGenerator.js`: `movieFirstLetter` (`Starts with "X"` — strips a leading "The"/"A"/"An" first so a low-quality "starts with T" bucket doesn't get dominated by every "The ..." title; skips titles starting with a digit/symbol rather than mis-bucketing them) and `movieIsSingleWordTitle` (`One-word titles`). **Deliberately NOT implemented: syllable-count categories** — accurate English syllable counting needs a real dictionary/library to be reliable; a naive heuristic would misclassify often enough to undermine trust in the puzzle, so this was scoped out rather than shipped unreliable.
- **GamesHub + Connections + Six Degrees now offer "help me get started" quick-pick suggestions on their data-gate screens (Jul 2026, bug report — "new users really won't have access to [games]... offer them movies... expand their list").** All three reuse the exact same `NewRatingSearch` component (`suggestionsMode`) Home.vue's new-user onboarding already uses — 3 shuffled popular TMDB movies, tap to jump into `RateMovie`. Reused as-is rather than building a games-specific version. Scoped to these three (not Wordle/Higher-or-Lower, whose bare-minimum data needs — 1 and 2 movies respectively — don't really hit this wall the way Connections/Six Degrees' much larger real-world data appetite does). Each host component needed `axios` mocked in its own test file for the first time, since `NewRatingSearch`'s `mounted()` fetches TMDB popular movies — it already fails gracefully into an empty list without a mock (defensive `try/catch`), so nothing broke, but an explicit mock avoids a real (harmlessly-failing) network attempt during tests.
- **Six Degrees difficulty, v1 → a real weighted composite (Jul 2026, user idea, then immediate follow-up).** First pass used hop count alone (`DIFFICULTY_LEVELS` as a fixed 2/3/4-hop relabeling), deliberately skipping billing/year/age since "the user themselves was unsure how those would even combine into one score." The user then directly asked whether those were considered and, given a clearer steer, asked for them to be included — as smaller-weighted factors, not equal partners to hops. Replaced with a genuine weighted score in `sixDegrees.js`:
  - **`scorePathDifficulty(path, entriesByKey)`** — 0 (easiest) to 1 (hardest), combining four normalized components: hop count (weight 0.4), billing order (0.4 — for each person-step, the WORSE/higher-index of their billing across the two movies they connect, averaged across the path; reads `movie.cast` directly for the exact index, not the truncated `movieCastNames`), year gap between the two endpoint movies (0.1 — the user's own "50 years apart is really hard" pinned the normalization cap), and average age of the two endpoint movies (0.1 — "older movies will be harder," a smaller factor per the user's explicit ranking).
